**Output:** spec first (spec/cli.md §5.14): render publishes a decision, question or Document only when its lifecycle is `reviewed`, `disputed` or `archived` (a whole Document is reviewed from creation), and a person whatever its lifecycle; the relations that point at an unpublished note (Inbox Produced, Meeting Decisions and Questions, Supersedes) wait as pending relations; approving a Review Notes row publishes its notes in the same run; a rejected draft never gets a page; what happens to existing Draft pages (left until decided, or trashed after a yes); Briefs, reports and Review pages unchanged; then the CLI, brain-sync/catch-up wording, the Notion design and views (drop "Which concepts are drafts?" and other Draft views), and a migration.
**Acceptance:** tests: a draft decision gets no page and its meeting's Decisions relation fills in when it is approved; a rejected draft never renders; a draft person still renders; a disputed decision keeps its page; checks as CC-123.
**Answers (the user, 2026-11-04):** 1. Existing Draft pages: moved to Notion's trash after a yes (migration 0020, `sync render --unpublish-drafts`), only those a pending Review notes row holds, so approving brings each back as a new page. 2. A decision a connected workflow publishes arrives `reviewed` (the approval in the session is the review, as for a workflow Document), with `workflow: {id, artifact}` on the note. Following 0071 §3 without a new question: an approved `new_item` writes its note `reviewed`.

### CC-126 · A to-do becomes a task, not a question (decision 0071) · `done` (#209)
**Release:** 0.9.1
**Changelog:** the brain no longer files to-dos as questions: your own become tasks, other people's stay commitments you can track as Waiting On, and questions are kept for what something can answer later; a one-off tidy offers to turn existing to-do questions into tasks.
**Output:** spec first, in the contracts that hold the rule today: spec/capture-and-review.md §4.1 and §6 and spec/cli.md §5.14.5 (unchanged for other people's commitments: Waiting On only through `Track: Waiting On —`, decision 0055), then skills/brain-ingest/references/extraction.md, sources.md and selection.md (answerable later → question; the owner's explicit to-do → task; someone else's → a commitment in the text; ambiguous owner or tentative idea → no task and no question; risk → Kind Risk), the minutes format's Open Questions and Risks guidance (notion/minutes-format.md), and a tidy (in brain-tidy or a cleanup row: the spec decides) that lists existing questions that read as the owner's to-dos and converts each after a yes, dropping the question; then the skills and their mirror, and a blind run on synthetic minutes.
**Spec questions (from the review of PR #204):** 1. Old-format minutes (Open Questions as plain lines, no item ids): does the rule apply to them, and how is a converted line tied to its task without an item id? 2. Idempotence: a re-ingest must not create a second task for a line that was a question before; is `task_items` enough for an item that changes kind? 3. The tidy: does dropping a converted question archive it with a successor link to the task, or simply archive it?
**Answers (the user, 2026-11-04):** 1. The rule applies to old-format minutes too: a line is classified by what it says, whatever its heading; an old-format Task goes through `sync tasks --meeting`, is recorded in the meeting's `tasks` and needs no item id, and a corrected re-read reads only the changed lines (`source diff`), so an unchanged line never makes a second Task. 2. A re-ingest never reclassifies a line the brain already holds as a question (open, answered or dropped): it creates no Task for it, and only the tidy converts it; a line the user edited is a corrected line, its Task is made once (`task_items`), and the old question is named under Check to drop. 3. A converted question is archived with `task` (the Task's page id), the meeting or source records the Task, and Notion gets Lifecycle Archived and Status Dropped, each sent once. 4. The tidy is a chat step of brain-ingest, run when the user asks ("turn my to-do questions into tasks"), each item after a yes, through a new `brain question drop`; a question that reads as someone else's commitment is offered as a drop with no Task (never Waiting On, 0055).
**Acceptance:** a blind run by a fresh Sonnet sub-agent on synthetic minutes with one real question, one owner to-do, one to-do for someone else (unmarked, then marked `Track: Waiting On —`), one ambiguous-owner line and one risk produces one question, one task, no task then one Waiting On task, nothing for the ambiguous line, and one risk; with Tasks off the owner's to-do stays in the text and is not a question; a re-ingest creates no second task; the tidy converts a to-do question only after a yes; the skill mirror check and the leak check pass.

### CC-136 · brain upgrade rewrites concept links inside proposals (dogfood #220) · `done` (#227)
**Release:** 0.9.2
**Changelog:** `brain upgrade` also moves the links to old concept notes that sit inside proposals, so no proposal points at `knowledge/concepts/` after 0.9.1.
**Output:** #220: the 0.9.1 concept conversion (spec/cli.md §5.15 step 5a, `planConceptConversion` in src/commands/upgrade.ts) rewrites links in note bodies but not inside the frontmatter strings of proposals in `_proposals/` (`content:` and the other text fields). Spec first: whether applied proposals are rewritten too or kept as history (pending ones must be, or they fail on apply); then the CLI test-first; an upgrade run on an already-converted brain fixes the links it missed (the step is safe to run again).
**Acceptance:** tests on a synthetic brain: a pending proposal whose `content:` links a concept points at its Document after the upgrade and still applies; applied proposals follow the spec's rule; running the upgrade again on a converted brain fixes missed links and changes nothing else; `brain lint` is clean; the existing upgrade tests pass.
**Spec questions (CC-136, for the user):** 1. Applied and closed proposals: are their links rewritten too, or kept as history? 2. A pending `update_body` whose target's body the conversion rewrites: does its `before_hash` follow, or does it go stale? 3. On a brain already converted the old paths are not recorded: how does a second upgrade find where a dead `knowledge/concepts/` link points? 4. A pending `new_item` whose note is a concept fails on apply: is it converted too?
**Answers (the user, 2026-11-05):** 1. Every proposal is rewritten, whatever its status (Git keeps the old text). 2. `before_hash` follows the rewritten body when it matched the old one; a proposal already stale stays stale. 3. By file name: the learned Document with a `con-` id at `knowledge/references/<name>.md`, else the only one at `<name>-<n>.md`; none or several leaves the link with warning `CONCEPT_LINK_UNRESOLVED`; the repair reads note bodies too. 4. Yes: a `new_item` not yet applied whose note is a concept becomes a learned Document.

### CC-118 · `brain repo refresh` adopts a kit for a legacy link and takes a new path · `done` (#224)
**Release:** 0.9.2
**Changelog:** a link made before kits takes its new kit, and a moved repo its new path, with `brain repo refresh`, without the brain listing the repo's files again.
**Output:** CC-104's spec questions 1 and 2 (answered 2026-11-04): spec/linked-repos.md and spec/cli.md §5.29 first (`repo refresh` applies a written kit to a legacy entry, and takes `--path` for a moved repo, both keeping `commit`; their checks and errors), then the CLI test-first in `src/commands/repo*`; brain-repos stops unlinking and linking again for these two cases, and its mirror.
**Acceptance:** tests on synthetic repos: a refresh of a legacy entry with a kit written applies the kit and keeps `commit`, and the next `repo changes` lists nothing again; a refresh with `--path` of a moved repo does the same; the existing repo tests pass; the skill mirror check passes.
**Spec decided (CC-118):** `repo refresh` never changes `commit`; a legacy entry adopts the kit in its repo, and stays `NO_KIT` when there is none and no `--path`; `--path` takes the checks of `repo add` plus the recorded commit, which must be in the repo at the new path and an ancestor of its `HEAD` (`REPO_COMMIT_UNKNOWN`), so a wrong repo is refused; `data` keeps its shape, `changed` also true for a new path; the log adds `path=` (cli §5.29, linked-repos §1, §5, §6, §9, §10).

## 0.10.0 — The brain assistant (decision 0064)

An assistant you ask, from the CLI, about the structure of your work: Areas, Domains, Projects and Tasks. It finds what's stalled, misplaced or missing, recommends, turns your thinking into captures, tasks and projects, and makes changes through Review rows, or directly after your yes in a live chat. On demand; the weekly scheduled run is CC-111 in 0.13.0. Starts after 0.9.0 is released, with its opening scope grill. The grill ran on 2026-11-05 (CC-137): decision [0072](../decisions/0072-ultimate-assistant.md) names it **Ultimate Assistant**, shows a report first and applies what you say yes to, with Review rows only when you ask.

**Plan (2026-11-06, CC-139):** the main session writes CC-108; beside it, build agents take CC-140 (fixtures), CC-141 (history move) and CC-138 (`main` requires `merge-ready`). Once CC-108 is on `main`, CC-109 is split into slices along its contracts and built in parallel; CC-110 runs on CC-140's fixtures.

## 0.11.0 — The brain tidies itself (decisions 0052, 0061, 0064, 0076)

Reflection across notes: contradictions, stale facts, duplicates, promotions and recurring themes, shown first in the chat; what the user says yes to is applied, "later" becomes a Review row, and nothing changes silently (0076). On demand only; the weekly run is 0.13.0's brief (CC-111). Candidates follow `brain query`'s restricted-evidence rule (0055). The agency brain's move-in comes after 1.0 and no longer gates it (0076).

### CC-65 · Contracts for reflection (decision 0052) · `done` (#257)
**Release:** 0.11.0
**Changelog:** contracts for reflection: candidates, the proposal fingerprint, and three new lint warnings.
**Output:** spec/cli.md: `brain reflect --candidates` (its four signals, the window, which lifecycles each signal reads, the cluster JSON, how fingerprints already on rejected or pending proposals are left out); the `orphan`, `note-size` and `near-duplicate` warnings in §5.10, with the name-matching rule; §6's reflection sequence: each finding's proposal kind, the cap of 10 rows and its order, a theme's draft and its Review notes row, the report, unattended. spec/schema.md: `fingerprint` on proposals and how it is formed. The unattended table of cli.md §6.1 becomes the one authority for what an unattended run may do: capture-and-review §8's selected-input rows move into it (§8 keeps a pointer), and the skills' tables are renderings of it (assessment finding F-0007). Per decision 0076: the chat flow (findings ranked top 10 then "more", the yes, later and skip answers and the proposal each records, a contradiction asking which value holds), the skip rule per kind and how the fingerprint records it (a merge stays skipped; a fact, contradiction or promotion returns on a newer source; a theme on a higher source count), the restricted-evidence rule of §5.13 in `reflect --candidates`, a theme as a draft learned Document, and the `note-size` scope; no unattended reflection rows (on demand only in 0.11.0), so §6's reflection sequence is the attended one.
**Acceptance:** the reviewer (or Codex) finds CC-66 and CC-67 buildable from the contracts with no spec question; every point of 0076 has its place in the contracts; schema examples validate for a `merge` and a `contradiction` proposal carrying a fingerprint.

### CC-66 · `brain reflect --candidates`, fingerprints and the new lint warnings (decision 0052) · `done` (#260)
**Release:** 0.11.0
**Changelog:** `brain reflect --candidates` finds likely duplicates, overtaken notes, notes that may disagree and recurring terms; proposals carry a fingerprint; lint warns on orphans, oversized notes and near-duplicate names.
**Output:** per CC-65: the command (with `brain query`'s restricted-evidence rule), `fingerprint` in `brain proposal new` and its validation, the skip rule per kind, the three lint warnings (`note-size` checks `learned` and `promoted` Documents and skips sources and `whole`, `part` and `workflow` ones, 0076), the code and tests.
**Acceptance:** tests on the fixture brain: two people "María López" and "M. Lopez" give a near-duplicate candidate and a `near-duplicate` warning; once a `merge` with their fingerprint is rejected, `--candidates` no longer gives the pair; a reviewed person note older than two meetings naming her gives an overtaken candidate; a term in three sources with no note gives a recurring-term candidate, and in two it doesn't; a 12 KB learned Document gives `note-size`, a 12 KB source or `whole` Document doesn't; a skipped contradiction stays held (in its cluster's `held`) until an input not in its `evidence`, created on or after the skip, names its note (spec/schema.md §11.4; whether that input bears on the field is the skill's check, CC-67); a restricted note is neither evidence nor a target; a learned Document nothing references and with no project or area gives `orphan`.

### CC-158 · Test temp folders are removed without ENOTEMPTY flakes · `done` (#261)
**Release:** 0.11.0
**Changelog:** development only: the tests turn off Git's background maintenance and retry removing their temp folders, so `npm test` no longer fails at random with ENOTEMPTY.
**Output:** on 2026-11-08 `npm test` failed three times with `ENOTEMPTY` while removing a temp brain's `.git/objects` (tests/review-apply.test.ts twice in a release run, tests/workflow.test.ts in #259's review, also on a copy of `main`). The cause: since Git 2.5x every commit starts a detached `git maintenance run --auto --detach` (seen with `GIT_TRACE`), which can still write into `.git` while the test removes the folder. tests/helpers.ts sets `maintenance.auto=false` and `gc.auto=0` through `GIT_CONFIG_*` for the test process, after any entries already set, so every git a test starts, directly or through the in-process CLI, inherits it; `fixtureCopy` and the package root's removal retry (`maxRetries`). Tests only: no product code.
**Acceptance:** tests/test-git-env.test.ts: a commit traced with `GIT_TRACE` starts no `maintenance run`, and the settings land after existing `GIT_CONFIG_*` entries; the full `npm test` passes three runs in a row; `npm run typecheck` and the leak check pass.

**Acceptance:**
- [ ] Two notes whose only common references are a meeting, a source, a domain, an area, a goal or a project, plus one other reference, are no `shared_ground` cluster; with two other references in common they are, and `shared` lists only those (test `common ground leaves out the meeting and the project…`).
- [ ] A term is never built from a link target, a URL, an email, a domain or file name, inline code, a line repeated in two or more window inputs, a one-character token, a contraction or term stopword at its edge, or a token of a person's name; a true term still clusters (test `a term never comes from link targets…`).
- [ ] spec/cli.md §5.32 defines every rule above, and its term stopword list equals the code's.
- [ ] `npm test`, `npm run typecheck` and the leak check pass.

## 0.12.0 — Remember and retrieve reliably (decisions 0061, 0074)

The opening grill ran on 2026-11-06 and is locked in [0074](../decisions/0074-scoped-memory-handovers-and-bounded-recall.md). Deliver six capabilities for Claude Code and Codex: hot opening context, cross-session work handovers, durable memory capture, targeted recall, scoped/correctable memories and reviewed promotion. Durable lessons stay in the existing brain memories; temporary progress stays in repo/task/session records; hot context is a generated selection of those records.

Work packages: contracts and a fixed representative synthetic retrieval/answer evaluation, starting from the existing baseline (`eval/search-0.5`, made runnable at the branch point with a `brain.json` in its generated brain and its recall/MRR numbers committed before any retrieval change; assessment proposal C2); closing capture (at most five suggestions plus explicit remember requests), approval and promotion through existing flows; work-record handovers and scoped opening recall with an initial 800-token budget; small deterministic retrieval adaptations, preferring claude-obsidian components over a new runtime and using agent-memory/obsidian-second-brain as references; both-agent acceptance. No blanket expiry, automatic full-note expansion or background memory model work. Gates: 100% of critical trust cases pass, at least 90% of answerable cases return correct, sufficiently complete cited answers, and opening/later retrieval costs are reported separately. Existing architecture, source restrictions, workflow boundaries and the 0.14.0 cutoff remain. Claude Code decomposes implementation after contracts; this scope record starts none.

### CC-165 · The 0.12.0 evaluation set: trust, answerable and unknown cases on a synthetic brain (0074 §6) · `done` (#273)
**Release:** 0.12.0

Each case names its expected evidence ids and the facts a correct answer must contain. A scorer computes the 0074 §6 gates (100% of trust cases, at least 90% of answerable cases, unknown cases scored separately) from a retriever's or answerer's output through a small adapter, independent of the command shapes CC-166 defines; the adapter for the current `brain query --candidates` is included as the first one. A README states the scoring rules. Fixtures are synthetic; no change outside `eval/memory-0.12/` and this row.
**Acceptance:** the generator is deterministic (two runs, identical bytes) and its brain lints with no errors; at least 15 trust, 40 answerable and 8 unknown cases, every kind of 0074 §6 covered; the scorer runs end to end with the `query --candidates` adapter and prints the gate results (passing them is not required: this is the baseline); the leak and PR format checks pass.

## 0.13.0 — Trust repair: answers and proposals keep approvals, reach and exclusions (decision 0077)

An independent assessment of `v0.12.0` (2026-11-09 and 2026-11-10; records outside this repo) found that native Claude Code and Codex sessions reached memories and statements the CLI withholds by opening the brain's files (F-0011), that memory rows in `query` did not follow recall's rules (F-0017) and that an approved proposal could write excluded wording into a note (F-0018). Fixes started on branches as 0.12.1 (CC-176 to CC-180); none has shipped. The user's grill of 2026-11-10 is locked in [0077](../decisions/0077-trust-repair-in-0-13-0.md): one operational eligibility rule (approval, reach under 0009, exclusion under 0055) for search, full read, summaries, citations and the evidence of proposals; a whole item withheld when its eligible support can't be established; superseded memories only in an explicit historical read; passages named and checked on every proposed change; one coordinated upgrade of the brain and its linked projects; one native run per agent on the final package. Operational eligibility only: no filesystem security product, no deletion from Git history or Notion sources, no ranking changes, no new features, no operation on the user's brain.

**Retrieval quality, added by [0080](../decisions/0080-english-retrieval-quality-in-0-13-0.md)** (the user's grill of 2026-11-11, amending 0077's ranking exclusion): for English questions, the **first** `brain query --candidates` finds supporting evidence in its first ten results for at least 18 of 20 body and structured facts and 18 of 20 paraphrases, nothing found today is lost, critical trust stays 100% and at least 90% of answerable final answers are correct and cited; an unresolved subject gets a short clarifying question; median and 95th-percentile latency and output tokens each grow by at most 25% over a matched trust-safe baseline. Eighty new audited cases; the original 50 unchanged. The contract is [spec/retrieval.md](../spec/retrieval.md). Not in scope: other languages, embeddings, a persisted index, model calls, a higher limit or retries as the success mechanism, any change to the eligibility rule.

| Task | Scope under 0080 | Owns | Starts | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC-202 | Decision 0080, the retrieval contract, this plan | `decisions/0080`, `spec/retrieval.md`, pointer lines, these rows | now | `done` (#305) |
| CC-203 | The eighty cases, their audit, the scorer's two levels, the cost tool and a provisional baseline | `eval/retrieval-0.13/` (new) | after CC-202 is merged (stacked on it until then) | `done` (#310) |
| CC-204 | Compare C1 to C4 alone, then the smallest useful combinations | `eval/retrieval-0.13/compare/` and its results file | after CC-203 | `done` (#318) |
| CC-205 | The chosen changes in `brain query --candidates`, spec first | cli.md §5.13, `src/lib/query.ts` and any new module it needs, their tests | after CC-204 is merged (CC-176 and CC-183, the trust-safe search, merged as #295 and #300) | `done` (#320) |
| CC-206 | Moved to 1.0.0 by 0082, with CC-186 (the 1.0.0 section) | — | — | `todo` |
| CC-186 | Moved to 1.0.0 by 0082: the answer-level gates wait for its run there; 0.13.0 is judged on §6.2's retrieval-level and cost gates (CC-204, CC-205) | — | — | `todo` |

One `pr-reviewer` at a time across both streams. CC-205 bases its numbers on the matched baseline (retrieval.md §7): `main` after the trust-safe search is merged, measured again if a later merge changes the search code.

**Plan (CC-181, 2026-11-10).** Contracts come first; every task conforms to `spec/eligibility.md` (CC-182). A task edits only what it owns plus its own row; one `pr-reviewer` at a time; a spec question goes to the main session, which owns `spec/`. Statuses below are as of `main` at 033ca6b; none of the reviews has a pass on its current head.

**The release gate, changed by [0082](../decisions/0082-native-smoke-run-per-release-full-native-acceptance-before-1-0.md)** (the user's answers of 2026-11-11): 0.13.0 is cut once every 0.13.0 task is merged, after the automated checks and a **native smoke run** of 15 cases per agent from `eval/memory-0.12` (every critical-trust kind, two answerable, one unknown) on the package of the head to be released, as [docs/releasing.md](../docs/releasing.md) says; every critical-trust case and every session's no-file-read check must pass, answerable and unknown are reported. The complete native acceptance (CC-186, with CC-206's eighty retrieval cases) moves to the 1.0.0 section, run once beta testers are live and the core has no critical revision left. The smoke run is a release step, not a task.

**What CC-176 to CC-180 take on when reconciled.** Their rows live only on their branches (CC-176 and CC-178 on #283's branch, CC-177, CC-179 and CC-180 on #284's). Each branch keeps its row text, its "After the review" notes and its tests, and adds:
- **CC-176:** a superseded memory only through the explicit historical read, labelled historical (0077 §2), and current questions get current eligible memories; titles, summaries, aliases, frontmatter and custom fields, citation labels and paths in `--read` and `--candidates` carry no restricted text; `brain index --json` and other listings are administrative and never answer evidence (its spec question 2, settled by CC-182's rule). Its spec questions 1 and 3 are answered by 0077: the release is 0.13.0, and it ships with the skills vendored by CC-180.
- **CC-177:** an item that cites an excluded passage beside a live one is withheld whole (skipped on create, closed on apply) and can be re-proposed from live evidence, replacing "only a text whose every cited line is excluded is skipped"; a fix or edit that changes the wording needs fresh exact acceptance; spec question 4 (an uncited restatement) is settled by CC-182 under 0077 §1 and §3. Questions 1 to 3 are answered by 0077 and this plan.
- **CC-179:** its open questions are settled by 0077 §3: every proposal names its passages, checked at creation and before apply; a proposal written before the field is legacy, withheld and re-proposed; in an older-layout `review_notes` approved whole, a folded change without established support is withheld, reported and queued for re-proposal while independent folded changes apply; a proposal's why, value and metadata carry no excluded text; a separately attributed user correction (0055) is kept, and a generic Approve or incidental edit does not carry an excluded claim through.
- **CC-178:** the fork range becomes `>=0.13.0-0 <0.14.0`; past questions use the historical read; no raw read or listing as a fallback. The 77-case native rerun leaves its acceptance for CC-186; the fork's checks and review stay.
- **CC-180:** answering's neighbours too: report gathering and refresh, ingest context, reflection, delegated catch-up and any other flow that reads existing brain content, listed in CC-182's contract, read through the filtered commands; reading the new input being ingested and administrative lookups (by `source_key`, `extract_of`, file hash) stay distinct. Its open question on `--read`'s limits for reflection is settled in CC-182. Then one fork release with CC-178 and CC-180, tagged with the user's yes, vendored with `scripts/vendor-skills.mjs`.

**Reconciling the branches with `main`, so no row is lost.** This PR writes no row of CC-176 to CC-180. When a branch next merges `main` after CC-181 is merged, git may merge `build/TASKS.md` without a conflict and leave a `## 0.12.1` section beside this one, so the author reconciles it by hand in the same task worktree:
1. Move the branch's rows under this section, after CC-181's row, in task order; set each `**Release:**` to 0.13.0; add the scope above to each row's Output and Acceptance; mark spec questions answered by 0077.
2. Delete the `## 0.12.1` heading and intro; the evidence they held is in 0077's Context.
3. The second branch to reconcile keeps every row from both sides (AGENTS.md) and one copy of each id: `grep -cE '^### CC-(17[6-9]|180) '` counts five, and `grep -nE '^(## 0\.12\.1|\*\*Release:\*\* 0\.12\.1)' build/TASKS.md` finds nothing.
4. Then CC-182's contract: the branch changes its spec text where `spec/eligibility.md` says so, then its code, then asks for a review of the new head.
If a branch is dropped instead, the main session writes the rows it carried on `main`, with the same ids, before any task that depends on them starts. The fork PR #12 follows CC-178's row.

### CC-176 · An eligible read for answers (brain query --read), and memory rows in query follow recall's rules (assessment F-0011, F-0017) · `done` (#295)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** `brain query --read <id>` gives an agent a note's full text with what you excluded, haven't approved or scoped elsewhere left out, so answers no longer need to open the brain's files; `brain query --types memory` now returns only the memories `brain recall` would. A memory that was replaced is read only with `brain query --read <id> --historical`, and a note with an excluded line is returned to agents without its title, path, tags or headings, cited by its type and id until its cleanup is done. After upgrading, run `brain link` again in each linked repo: its copy of the instructions and of brain-query changes only then.
**Output:** cli.md §5.13 (usage, step 2's memory rule) and the new §5.13.1; memory.md §2 ("One rule for every command and every answer") and §6.1; `brain query --read <id>[,<id>…] [--repo <path>]`: each note as the restricted-evidence rule leaves it (`frontmatter`, `body`, `restricted`), a memory only when it is eligible for the caller or is one `recall --search --include-superseded` returns, a proposal never, a withheld note as `{ id, status: "withheld", reason }` with `unknown`, `restricted` or `not_eligible`, exit 0; `brain query --candidates --types memory` searches only memories eligible for the caller (`shownMemories` in `src/lib/recall.ts`, the one reader both commands use); the `How:` paragraph `brain link` writes and rule 2 of `templates/brain/AGENTS.md` name the read and say a file found another way is never a source for an answer. No change to ranking. Synthetic fixtures only.
**Acceptance:** tests on the synthetic eligibility brain of `tests/recall.test.ts` cover: `query --types memory` returns exactly the memories `recall --search` may return to a linked folder and to a folder without `.brain`, and the default types still hold no memory; `--read` returns an eligible memory whole, a partly restricted decision with the excluded line as spaces and no `summary`, a superseded memory in reach with `superseded_by`, and withholds a draft, an out-of-reach and a restricted memory, a wholly restricted decision, a proposal and an unknown id, with no excluded text anywhere in the output; every `USAGE` case of §5.13.1; the `link` block tests hold with the new paragraph. `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass. After the review of #283: `tests/report-excluded.test.ts` covers a report that links a note resting on excluded content (read while the evidence is live; withheld `restricted` and no longer a `--types view` candidate after the exclusion) and an index with no alias of a wholly restricted note. Added by 0077: `tests/recall.test.ts` covers a superseded memory withheld by `--read` and returned with `--historical` labelled `historical: true` (not a draft, an out-of-reach or a restricted one), every other id read the same with and without the option, current reads and searches with no superseded memory, `--historical` with `--candidates` as `USAGE`, and `index --json` listing a draft memory that `--read` withholds; `tests/answer-metadata.test.ts` covers the awaiting-cleanup form in `--read` (no title, path, tags, aliases, summary or free-text field, headings and comments blank but `## Decision` and `## Sources`, line numbers held, `cite`), in `--candidates` (no withheld part scores or snips; a note with only its summary dropped keeps its title), in `recall --pack`, `--search` and `query --types memory`, and in `reflect --candidates` (no `near_duplicate` and no naming by a withheld title; a member and an input shown with `cite` and no title or path), a note whose only restricted claim is a timeline entry, or the entry behind a fact, in the same form, and a note restricted whole withheld with only its id and reason; each test asserts that no word of the excluded paragraph or the withheld title appears in the output.
**After the review (#283):** a view is returned whole or withheld (`eligibleView` in `src/lib/suppressions.ts`; cli.md §5.13.1, "Views", with its stated limit); `publishableNote` drops `aliases` on a wholly restricted note, so `index.md` and Notion no longer show them; rule 2 of `templates/brain/AGENTS.md` no longer sends the agent to `index.md`; §5.13.1 names `SUPPRESSIONS_UNREADABLE` and "20 distinct ids"; memory.md §2's sentence is narrowed to `recall` and `query`.
**After the second review (#283):** a report whose `generated_at` is earlier than an extract version that carries a suppression record is withheld until it is refreshed, which closes the case of a report line that restates excluded text and links no note; a relative link in a view's body that names no note withholds the view, as §5.13.1 said; §5.13.1 says a view stays withheld while a note it rests on is restricted, and names the one `index-stale` warning after upgrading (`tests/report-excluded.test.ts`).
**After the third review (#283):** only a link to a Markdown file (`.md`) that names no note and no file of the brain, or leaves the brain, withholds a view; a link to a PDF, an image, a folder or a `README.md` does not. A report's `generated_at` must be a full timestamp later than the excluded extract's `captured`: the same second, or a date alone on either side, withholds it (cli.md §5.13.1, "Views"; `tests/report-excluded.test.ts`, the last two tests).
**Added by 0077 (reconciled 2026-11-10, not built yet):** a superseded memory only through the explicit historical read, labelled historical (0077 §2), and current questions get current eligible memories only; titles, summaries, aliases, frontmatter and custom fields, citation labels and paths in `--read` and `--candidates` carry no restricted text; `brain index --json` and other listings are administrative and never answer evidence. Each follows `spec/eligibility.md` (CC-182): the spec text first, then the code, then a review of the new head. Acceptance adds a test for each.
**Added by 0077 (built):** spec text first (eligibility.md §13 rows 1, 2, 3 and 14): cli.md §5.13 defines the **awaiting-cleanup form** (a note with a restricted claim, a blanked body line or a dropped timeline entry or fact, has no `title`, `path`, `tags`, `summary`, `aliases` or free-text frontmatter, its headings and comments are spaces except a decision's fixed sections, nothing withheld adds a term, and it carries `cite`: `<type> <id>, title withheld: awaiting cleanup`); a note whose only change is a dropped summary has no restricted claim and keeps its title (eligibility.md §6 rule 3, last sentences); §5.13.1 adds `--historical` (`USAGE` with `--candidates`), withholds a superseded memory `not_eligible` without it and labels one read with it `"historical": true`; §5.32 and memory.md §5.3 and §6.1 apply the same form to reflection and recall; the `How:` paragraph `brain link` writes says to cite by `cite` and read a replaced memory only with `--historical`. Code: `answerNote`, `titleWithheld` and `citeLabel` in their own section of `src/lib/suppressions.ts` (the published surfaces keep `eligibleNote` and `publishableNote` for CC-183), used by `src/commands/query.ts`, `src/lib/query.ts`, `src/lib/recall.ts` and `src/lib/reflect.ts`. `brain index --json` is unchanged and stays administrative (eligibility.md §7).
**Spec questions:** (1) and (3) answered by 0077: the release is 0.13.0, and it ships with the skills vendored by CC-180; the weekly review (CC-111) moves to 0.14.0 (0077 §6; now 0.15.0, 0078). (2) `brain index --json` lists every memory, drafts included, with title and summary: settled by CC-182's rule that a listing is administrative and never answer evidence. (4) **Answered (b) by the user on 2026-11-11; built in CC-183:** a note in the awaiting-cleanup form loses its `path`, but another note's live body line can still link to its file, whose name repeats the title (a meeting line `See [the decision](../decisions/2026-10-11-budget-cut-to-40k.md)` is returned whole). Neither 0077 nor eligibility.md decides it. Options: (a) a stated limit in cli.md §5.13 (the file name is the note's slug; the link is the other note's live text); (b) blank the link target, keeping the link text, in every answer-surface line that links a note in that form. Recommended: (b), as a follow-up in CC-183 or a CC-176 fix round. Not built here. (5) **Reading of eligibility.md §6 rule 3, confirmed by the user (2026-11-11):** the trigger for the awaiting-cleanup form is a restricted claim (a blanked body line, a dropped timeline entry or fact); a note whose only change is a dropped `summary` keeps its title, path and headings, after rule 3's last sentence ("A note with no restricted claim keeps its title"); written in cli.md §5.13 and tested.

### CC-177 · Apply closes a proposal that rests only on excluded evidence, and proposal new refuses one (assessment F-0018) · `done` (#293)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** A proposed change that rests on content you excluded, even beside content you kept, is no longer written to Review, and approving an older one closes it whole instead of adding any of its wording to a note; a value you type yourself is kept as your own correction.
**Output:** cli.md §5.6 (the `SOURCE_EXCLUDED` skip) and §5.7 ("Excluded evidence"), schema.md §11 (`closed_reason: stale` also covers it), capture-and-review.md §5.1's row; `restsOnlyOnExcluded` in `src/lib/suppressions.ts`, called by `applyDecision` (`src/lib/apply.ts`, before the kind is applied, on an approve of every kind except `review_notes` and `notion_change`) and by `stageProposals` (`src/lib/proposals.ts`); `tests/excluded-proposal.test.ts`. Not in this task: the folded changes of an older-layout `review_notes` approved as a whole (CC-179). A text with one excluded and one live statement was applied whole in the first part; 0077's added scope withholds it whole (below). Its rows sit at the head of the 0.13.0 section, where CC-176's branch puts its own with a lead-in line: keep both sets of rows when the second branch merges.
**Acceptance:** on the fixture brain with `tests/exclusion-fixture.ts`'s input: a pending `add_to_note` whose content cites only the excluded passage is approved in chat after the exclusion and comes back `closed`, `SOURCE_EXCLUDED`, with `closed_reason: stale`, the decision recorded and the target note unchanged, while one citing a live passage of the same input is applied in the same run; `proposal new` after the exclusion skips the excluded-passage item and an item with no passage reference whose source is the older version, and writes the items on a live passage, on the current version and on a mixed text; with no suppression record nothing is skipped or closed; `npm test` and the typecheck pass; the assessment's reproduction (an `add_to_note` on a "Meeting only" passage, approved in chat) ends with the target decision still returned by `brain query`. After the review of #284: a live claim beside an `^[inferred]` line or a heading is written and applied, and only a text whose every cited line is excluded is skipped; a `fix` of a `new_item`'s title is closed `SOURCE_EXCLUDED` while a fix that replaces the wording is not judged; the human output of a skip names its reason; on the new Review layout a change whose wording cites only an excluded passage is held and its wording not published, while one on a live passage is shown (`tests/excluded-proposal.test.ts`, `tests/review-page.test.ts`).
**After the review (#284):** `restsOnlyOnExcluded` judges only the lines that carry a passage reference; `applyDecision` checks a `fix` or `edit` that keeps the proposed text; `src/lib/review-body.ts` applies the same rule to `update_body` and `add_to_note` items of the new layout; `brain proposal new` prints each skip's reason; cli.md §5.7 says what the rule does not see, replacing the earlier "partly restricted" sentence.
**After the second review (#284):** the wording that would be written is judged, so a `fix` or `edit` whose value equals the proposal, or still cites a passage, is checked, and only a different value with no passage is the user's own (`applyDecision`; cli.md §5.7, "Which decisions are checked, and what is judged"); §5.7 names the uncited-restatement limit and the wording of an item published before the exclusion (`tests/excluded-proposal.test.ts`, the last test).
**Added by 0077 (built):** following `spec/eligibility.md` §4.2, §4.3, §9.2, §9.5 and §9.6 and §13 rows 6, 7, 9 (its half), 10 (the reason) and 11: cli.md §5.6, §5.7 ("Excluded evidence", "A `review_notes` with `items`", "Re-proposing from the live passages", "Which decisions are checked", "The user's own wording", "What the rule does not see"), §5.14.3 and §5.14.4, capture-and-review §5.1's row, schema §11 (`closed_reason`, `item_decisions`, §11.2's `update_fact`, `add_to_note` and `update_body`). `restsOnlyOnExcluded` becomes `restsOnExcluded` (`src/lib/suppressions.ts`): any cited line that is not live withholds the item. `applyDecision` (`src/lib/apply.ts`) checks a `review_notes` with `items` item by item on every route (chat, a row-level Approve), by the Review page's `source_excluded` (`withheldItems`): a withheld change or link is not applied, a withheld draft is archived, each is recorded in `item_decisions` (`reject`, `closed`, `source_excluded`), the rest applies, and the row closes `stale` when every pending item is withheld. The user's own value (differs, cites no passage) is applied as `user-correction:<prp id>`: an `update_fact` keeps the source's value as `superseded` (none when the proposal rests on excluded evidence), an `add_to_note` ends with `Source: user-correction:<id>`, and neither it nor an `update_body` adds the proposal's `source` to `sources`. `src/lib/review-body.ts`: a draft with a restricted line is `source_excluded`, so an Accept closes it; an item held only for its target still waits (`HELD_SOURCE_EXCLUDED`). Tests whose expectation the mixed rule reverses: `tests/excluded-proposal.test.ts` "proposal new skips a change that rests on excluded evidence, a mixed one whole" (the mixed text was kept) and the human skip message; the golden `update_fact-edit` (`tests/apply.test.ts`), whose edited value is now attributed `user-correction:prp-0a1b2c3d`.
**Acceptance added by 0077:** on the fixture brain with `tests/exclusion-fixture.ts`'s input, after the exclusion: `proposal new` skips a mixed text (a live and an excluded cited line) and writes the same change from its live passage alone; a mixed `add_to_note` approved in chat, its decision recorded before the exclusion, is closed `SOURCE_EXCLUDED` with `closed_reason: stale` and neither its excluded nor its live line reaches the note, while an independent live item applies in the same run; its re-proposal from the live passage is a new pending proposal that `apply` without `--input` leaves pending, then applies on its own Approve; a fix that keeps a mixed wording is closed and one that keeps only the live cited line applies; a fix and an edit the user typed that restate the excluded content apply with `Source: user-correction:<prp id>` and a timeline entry `user-correction:<prp id>`, without the excluded source in `sources` or the excluded value in the timeline; a new-layout `review_notes` approved in chat withholds its mixed change and mixed draft (dropped `SOURCE_EXCLUDED`, archived, `item_decisions` closed `source_excluded`) and applies its live change and link (`tests/excluded-proposal.test.ts`, the last five tests); on the Review page a mixed change is held with no wording or why (`tests/review-page.test.ts`, the last test), and an exclusion made after the render is caught by `sync apply`: an Accept closes a mixed change and a mixed draft and applies an independent item (`tests/review-apply.test.ts`, "an exclusion made after the render…"). `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.
**Spec questions:** (1) Answered by 0077: release 0.13.0, for CC-176 to CC-180. (2) Answered by decision 0077 §3: every proposed change names the passages it rests on, checked when it is created and before it is applied (CC-179 below). (3) Answered by 0077 and its plan: CC-179 and CC-180 are 0.13.0 tasks. (4) Settled by CC-182 (eligibility.md §9.6): an uncited restatement beside a live claim stays a stated limit, narrowed by passages on every change (CC-179), filtered reads (CC-180) and the ingest's writing rule (CC-180); cli.md §5.7, "What the rule does not see", says so. (5) Accepted by the user on 2026-11-10 (eligibility.md §9.5): a `fix` or `edit` value the user typed that restates excluded content without its passage reference is the user's own correction, applied as `user-correction:<prp id>`; a warning in the skill is optional.

After CC-176 is merged: the fork branch merged and released as a snapshot by the fork's own process, then vendored here (`scripts/vendor-skills.mjs`). `.agents/skills/` is not edited by hand.
**Added by 0077 (reconciled 2026-11-10):** past questions use CC-176's historical read; no raw read or listing as a fallback; the fork release with CC-180 is one release, vendored by CC-180. The 77-case native rerun below moves to CC-186's acceptance, on the final package.
**Acceptance:** the fork's checks pass on the branch (`tools/check-skills.mjs`, `check-names.mjs`, `npm test --prefix tools`, `leak-check.mjs`) and its review passes; at CC-186 (not this row), with the vendored snapshot on a brain from `eval/memory-0.12/make-brain.mjs`, the 77 cases asked as final answers in native Claude Code and Codex sessions, one per caller repo, give critical trust 20 of 20 on both when every answer is read against 0074 §6 (quoting a forbidden memory with a caveat counts as a failure), answerable at least 90%, and no session opens or greps a note file of the brain for an answer. The assessment's run of this on the two unmerged branches (2026-11-10, records outside this repo): 20 of 20 on both, answerable 46 of 47 and 47 of 47, no file read of the brain.
**Released:** the fork side is ultimate-brain-skills#12, released in `brain-v1.6.0` and vendored by #301 (CC-180). The 77-case native acceptance is CC-186's.

### CC-179 · Excluded evidence on proposals with no text, and on older-layout Review rows approved whole (found by the review of #284) · `done` (#298)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** Every proposed change now names the passages it rests on, so a fact change, link, merge or supersession that rests on content you excluded is closed instead of applied, as is an older proposal whose input has excluded content, and each item of a Review row written before 0.8.0 is judged on its own.
**Output:** to be specified first: a `passage` (or `passages`) field on a proposal, written by the ingest from the item it comes from, so `update_fact`, `contradiction`, `supersede`, `merge` and `add_link` can be judged by cli.md §5.7's rule when their `source` is the current version; the same check on each folded change of an older-layout `review_notes` approved as a whole. Open: whether the ingest skill can always name the passage, and what happens to proposals written before the field exists.
**Added by 0077 (reconciled 2026-11-10):** its open questions are settled by 0077 §3: every proposal names its passages, checked at creation and before apply; a proposal written before the field is legacy, withheld and re-proposed; in an older-layout `review_notes` approved whole, a folded change without established support is withheld, reported and queued for re-proposal while independent folded changes apply; a proposal's why, value and metadata carry no excluded text; a separately attributed user correction (0055) is kept, and a generic Approve or incidental edit does not carry an excluded claim through. Starts after CC-177 is merged, following `spec/eligibility.md` (CC-182).
**Acceptance:** an `update_fact` sourced on the current version whose value comes from an excluded paragraph is closed `SOURCE_EXCLUDED` on approve (it is applied at #284's head); an older-layout `review_notes` approved as a whole drops a folded `add_to_note` that cites only an excluded passage and applies the rest.
**Added by 0077 (built):** following `spec/eligibility.md` §4.1, §4.2, §4.3, §9.1 to §9.4 and §13 rows 8, 9 (its half) and 10 (the field): schema §11 (`passages`; `items[].passages`; `dropped`'s `source_excluded` entries; `closed_reason`; new §11.5 on where passages are, how a legacy proposal is recognised and how support is judged), `spec/schemas/proposal.schema.json`; cli.md §5.6 (`passages` required on every kind but `review_notes`, whose notes take `note_passages`, and `notion_change`, which takes none; each passage resolved; the text's references among them; `proposal list` lists a held item without its wording, title, path or why), §5.7 ("Excluded evidence": passages judged, legacy rule, each item of an older-layout row; a fix's own value only on the kinds whose value is the whole change; a fix value that cites passages rests on those), §5.14.3 and §5.14.4 (held rows on Notion, Name `Held: <id>`), §5.27 (`passages` on a revision); capture-and-review §5.1's row. Code: `passageLive` and `withheldSupport` in `src/lib/suppressions.ts`; `src/lib/passages.ts` (new: `proposalWithheld` per proposal and per item, `passageProblems`); `stageProposals` checks and stores passages (`src/lib/proposals.ts`, also `heldForm` for `proposal list`); `applyDecision` (`src/lib/apply.ts`) judges every route by them, judges an older-layout row item by item (`olderLayoutWithheld`, recorded in `dropped`, returned in `withheld`) and reports `input` and `legacy` on a `SOURCE_EXCLUDED` close; `reviewItems` (`src/lib/review-body.ts`) holds items by the same rule; `sync render` (`src/lib/sync.ts`) holds a withheld standalone or older-layout row; deferral and revision carry an item's passages. Eval hook: `PROPOSAL_PASSAGES_FIELD = "passages"` in `eval/memory-0.12/make-brain.mjs` (CC-185's ans-52). Tests whose expectation the rule changes: `tests/excluded-proposal.test.ts` "proposal new skips…" (the current version named as a whole input of an input with a suppression record is not live) and `tests/proposal.test.ts`'s stored file (its `passages` line); other suites' `proposal new` inputs get passages through `withPassages` in `tests/helpers.ts`.
**Acceptance added by 0077:** on the fixture brain with `tests/exclusion-fixture.ts`'s input (`tests/proposal-passages.test.ts`): an `update_fact` whose passage is the excluded paragraph is closed `SOURCE_EXCLUDED` on approve (`input` named) and the same change sourced on the current version is skipped by `proposal new`, while one on a live paragraph applies; an `add_link`, a `merge` and a `contradiction` resting on an excluded passage are closed and a live link applies; a fix of a merge's survivor is still judged; `proposal new` refuses an item with no or empty passages, a duplicate, an unknown paragraph or source, a text reference outside the list, a `review_notes` without `note_passages` or with an extra key, and accepts a whole input and an entry that names no input, storing an item's passages on its `items` entry; a legacy proposal applies when its input has no suppression record and is closed `SOURCE_EXCLUDED` with `legacy: true` and its input when it has one; an older-layout row approved whole drops only its change citing an excluded passage (recorded in `dropped`) and applies its live change, note and link, and one on an input with a suppression record withholds its folded change and link and only the draft citing the excluded paragraph, and reviews the other two drafts; on a 0.8.0-layout row an item with live passages applies and a legacy one on the excluded input is withheld; `proposal list` lists a held item without its wording, title, path or why, and a row's held change without its value; `sync render` names a held proposal `Held: <id>` with no excluded text. On a brain from `eval/memory-0.12/make-brain.mjs`, `brain apply` applies `prp:support-uptime` and `prp:quill-legacy` and closes the excluded, mixed and legacy ones; the reference adapter passes ans-51 and ans-52 with both gates met. `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.
**After the review (#298):** `tests/proposal-passages.test.ts` adds a legacy proposal sourced on the current version of an input with a suppression record (withheld by its input, `legacy: true`) and a stored text citing an excluded passage its `passages` do not name (closed): each fails when its line of `withheldSupport` is removed. For the user's answer of 2026-11-11 (Spec questions (1)): schema §11.5 and cli.md §5.7 judge a legacy note by its draft's own claims (body lines, timeline entries, facts; not the summary, which retrieval drops for any note on a suppressed input), and `proposalWithheld` (`src/lib/passages.ts`) reads it with `restrictionOf`; `tests/proposal-passages.test.ts`: an older-layout row with 3 drafts, one citing the excluded paragraph, withholds and archives only that one, and its folded change and link are withheld by the legacy rule; a 0.8.0-layout row with legacy items does the same for a draft and a link.
**Spec questions:** (1) Answered by the user on 2026-11-11, reading eligibility.md §9.3 for note items: a legacy note item of a `review_notes` (older layout, or a 0.8.0 to 0.12.x row with items) is judged by the draft's own claims, retrieval's rule: a restricted line, or uncited text resting on the suppressed input, makes it withheld (archived on approve); a draft whose own claims are all live stays reviewable. Changes and links, which have no claims of their own, keep the literal rule (the row's `source` as a whole-source entry). Built in the fix round below. (2) Settled here under eligibility.md, for the review: `notion_change` takes no passages (it changes Notion; cli §5.7 never judges it); a fix of a `merge`, `supersede`, `add_link` or `new_item` is judged as the proposal (§9.5, an incidental edit does not launder); `proposal new` lists every note of a `review_notes` whatever its passages hold, since the draft is already in the brain, and holds it on the Review page; a held proposal's `path` is left out of `proposal list` because it is made from the title.

### CC-180 · brain-ingest and brain-reflect read notes through the CLI (found by the review of ultimate-brain-skills#12) · `done` (#301)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** Ingest, tidying, catch-up and the assistant read what the brain holds only through `brain`, name the passages every change they propose rests on, and never write a statement from content you excluded.
**Output:** the skills fork: brain-ingest step 5 (the context load of up to eight notes) and brain-reflect `references/judging.md` read with `brain query --read`; brain-catch-up follows them. The frontmatter lookups by `source_key`, `extract_of` and file hash stay as they are (lookup, not content). Open: whether `--read`'s 20-id limit and its withholding of older source versions fit reflect, which compares versions.
**Added by 0077 (reconciled 2026-11-10):** answering's neighbours too: report gathering and refresh, ingest context, reflection, delegated catch-up and any other flow that reads existing brain content, listed in CC-182's contract, read through the filtered commands; reading the new input being ingested and administrative lookups (by `source_key`, `extract_of`, file hash) stay distinct. Its open question on `--read`'s limits for reflection is settled in CC-182. Then one fork release with CC-178 and CC-180, tagged with the user's yes, vendored with `scripts/vendor-skills.mjs`.
**Added by 0077 (built):** the fork's PR (branch `claude/CC-180-other-flows-read-through-the-cli`, stacked on CC-178's #12), every CC-180 flow of `spec/eligibility.md` §10, with the CLI range `>=0.13.0-0 <0.14.0` that #12 set and impact `coordinated` (it needs CC-176's `--read` and CC-179's `passages`). The product branch holds a `--local` snapshot of the fork head with `package.json` at 0.13.0 (skills-delivery §9.2, §9.4), replaced by the release. By skill:
- **brain-ingest:** a section "What you may read, and what you may write" keeps initial intake (the new input, `source extract-plan`, `source diff`), existing content (only `brain query --candidates` and `--read`; a withheld note is not loaded and nothing stands in for it; the awaiting-cleanup form compared on what it returns) and administrative lookups (by `source_key`, `extract_of`, `replaces`, file hash, a meeting's Notion page id: ids and paths only) apart, with no raw read, grep or listing as a fallback. Step 4 matches names with `--candidates --types`, step 5 loads its eight notes with one `--read` (the project's recent decisions and questions picked by id from `index --json`'s `backlinks`). The writing rule (eligibility §9.6 point 3): nothing from an excluded paragraph of the input being read, cited or not, in a note, a proposal's title, why, value or text, a summary, a digest, a task or a report line; a re-read of a selected input never repeats the excluded wording. Every proposal carries `passages` and a `review_notes` its `note_passages` (`references/cli-inputs.md` §3: an extract's `src-…#¶n:…`, else the input itself); a `SOURCE_EXCLUDED` skip is named under Check without its wording. Duplicate checks (a capture repeating a source, a learned Document, a question already held, a new version of a document), the promoted Document, a Document's new version, a `whole` Document of a source already in the brain and the to-do tidy read through `--read`; a note returned with `restricted: true` is not promoted or copied and waits, named under Needs you.
- **brain-reflect:** members and inputs read with `--read`, up to 20 ids a call, as many calls as the cluster needs; a withheld one is not judged; the awaiting-cleanup form is shown by its `cite` label; the survivor of a merge counted from `index --json`'s `backlinks`; every finding's proposal names its `passages` (line references, a timeline entry's `passage` or `source`, else the input or member id; a merge the two ids; a theme's draft its `note_passages`); `SOURCE_EXCLUDED` skips and closes are confirmed without wording.
- **brain-catch-up:** the rule holds in every step, sub-agent and unattended run; related knowledge, the duplicate check between inputs and the Documents kept from brain content read through the CLI.
- **ultimate-assistant:** scope and ramble names resolved with `--candidates`; a rule that note text comes only from `--read`; `set_field` and `merge` carry `passages` = the session key. **brain-apply:** a held proposal (`held: true`, CC-179) shown by its kind and target only, a `SOURCE_EXCLUDED` close or dropped item reported without wording, a change typed in What to fix carrying `user-correction:<prp id>`. **brain-howto:** a knowledge question goes to brain-query's reads. **brain-query** (what #12 left): the memory and report-fix proposals carry `passages` (the session key).
**Acceptance:** the fork's checks pass on its branch (`tools/check-skills.mjs`, `check-names.mjs`, `npm test --prefix tools`, `leak-check.mjs`); with the local snapshot, `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass. Blind runs by fresh sub-agents on a synthetic sandbox brain with the snapshot installed, a CLI built from a scratch clone of `main` with the passed branches of CC-176 (CC-183 merged into it) and CC-179 merged (their commits recorded), every CLI call with `--brain <sandbox>` and a failing `gh` stub: in a blind ingest of minutes that only refer to an excluded figure, and in a blind reflection over a brain with an exclusion, no session opens a note file under the brain, and no proposal's `why`, value or text holds the excluded statement. After the review: the fork release `brain-v<version>` tagged on the user's yes, vendored with `scripts/vendor-skills.mjs`, `--check --online` passing, and the release classified in the integration issue.
**Released:** `brain-v1.6.0` at fork commit 2441801. That is `main` after #12 and #15: #13 had merged into #12's branch, so #15 relanded the same head, cc99992, on `main`. It is vendored with `scripts/vendor-skills.mjs 1.6.0`, at the same integrity as the reviewed local snapshot (`sha256-eedfe90…`), and `--check --online` passes. It is classified `brain-work` in the integration issue (#306), with `assessed` at 1.6.0. The blind ingest and reflection ran on `main` at 6f4d323, with results on #301. The duplicate person notes that a withheld note causes in ingest become CC-207.

### CC-182 · Contracts for the trust repair: one eligibility rule for every read and every proposal's evidence (0077) · `done` (#287)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** development only: one contract says what an answer, a summary, a citation and a proposal may rest on, and how the upgrade and the final acceptance are judged.
**Output:** `spec/eligibility.md` (new): the operational eligibility rule (approval, reach under 0009, exclusion under 0055) applied the same way by supported search, full read, summaries, citations and a proposal's supporting evidence; the item (one indivisible change or one report) and whole-item withholding, with independent items and source and history files kept (0077 §1); the explicit historical read for superseded memories, labelled (§2); metadata that carries no restricted text (titles, summaries, aliases, frontmatter and custom fields, citation labels and paths); administrative access versus answering, a listing never being answer evidence; derived content (direct and indirect dependencies, freshness against an exclusion and its cleanup, fail closed, no "navigation only" exception); passages on every proposal, checked at creation and before apply, legacy proposals withheld and re-proposed, exact re-acceptance of changed wording, 0055's corrections (§3); the skill flows that read existing content, and what counts as initial intake or administrative work; the coordinated upgrade and its unfinished states (§4); the acceptance evidence and the rerun rule (§5); what the rule does not claim (filesystem security, semantic truth). One pointer line each in cli.md, memory.md §2, capture-and-review.md §5.1 and schema.md §11, and the release lines in cli.md (scheduled reflection) and assistant.md (the scheduled run, per-area preferences) moved to their releases under 0077 and 0078 (the scheduled run 0.15.0, per-area preferences 0.14.0). Command-level sections stay with CC-176, CC-177, CC-179, CC-183 and CC-184, which conform to this file.
**Acceptance:** each choice in 0077 §1 to §5 and each item of its plan has a rule that cites it and names the task that implements it; where spec text on CC-176's or CC-177's head differs, the file says which rule wins and which task changes the other; no new provenance store or platform is defined; the leak and PR format checks pass.
**Spec questions:** the user's choices of 2026-11-10 (eligibility.md): (1) a note with a restricted part stays readable without its title, path, tags and body headings, cited by type and id until its cleanup is decided (§6 rule 3; CC-176, CC-183); (2) a legacy proposal whose input has no suppression record has adequate passage support and applies normally (§9.3, the user's reading of 0077 §3). Settled in CC-182 under 0077 and reported to the user, 2026-11-10: the historical full read is `brain query --read … --historical` (§5); a draft knowledge note stays eligible, labelled by lifecycle (§2); `proposal list` marks a held item without its wording (CC-179); only repos registered in `brain.json` are upgraded (§11). Settled in the contract: CC-176's question 2 (§7), CC-177's question 4 (§9.6), CC-180's question on `--read` for reflection and its list of flows (§10).

### CC-183 · Derived content keeps eligibility: a report, view or index entry is withheld whole when its safety can't be shown (0077 plan item 3) · `done` (#299, #300)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** A report, view or index entry that rests, directly or through another report or view, on something you excluded, haven't approved or scoped elsewhere is withheld until it is rebuilt, even after its source is cleaned.
**Output:** spec/eligibility.md's derived-content rule, extending what CC-176 ships (a view read whole or withheld; a report older than an exclusion withheld): indirect dependencies (a report on a view on a note); freshness judged against the exclusion and its cleanup, so a report written before the exclusion stays withheld after its source is cleaned until it is refreshed; a report whose provenance or freshness can't be read is withheld whole; `index.md` and Notion publishing keep no restricted metadata; a withheld item names its reason and the refresh that rebuilds it; canonical notes supported independently stay readable. Files are decided from the contract (the view and report checks in `src/lib/suppressions.ts`, `src/lib/report-body.ts`, the index writer, a new test file, cli.md's view and index sections).
**Acceptance:** tests on a synthetic brain: a report that links a view resting on an excluded note is withheld; a report written before an exclusion stays withheld after the note is cleaned and is readable after a refresh; a report with unreadable provenance or freshness is withheld whole; a note supported by a live source stays readable; no title, alias or summary of a wholly restricted note appears in `index.md` or a view; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.
**Built:** spec text first: cli.md §5.13.1 "Views" (three causes in order: `dependency` to any depth through views, a cycle once, a memory as the caller would be shown it; `stale` against active **and reconciled** records; `unreadable` provenance or times; the entry adds `cause` and `rebuild`: `refresh` for a report, `views` for the others), §5.8 (a note restricted whole or with a restricted claim is listed in `index.md` as `- (title withheld: awaiting cleanup) — <id>` with its content-free fields and no link; in the JSON index `title`, `path` and `summary` `null`, `aliases` and `tags` `[]`; a withheld view's title and summary `null`; a source keeps its title), §5.9 and schema §16 (a view shows such a note as `<type> <id>, title withheld: awaiting cleanup`, unlinked and outside `generated_from`, so a regenerated view no longer rests on it), schema §13 (the index line), §5.14.4 "Restricted notes" (Name is that label, Summary and free text empty, the body in the awaiting-cleanup form; other notes named as text by the label on Review and Suggestion rows and Key Facts, and in a Review page's item sections (headings, target link, a link item's values; an Exclusion cleanup item keeps the title), a page already published getting it through `item_updates` reason `title_withheld` (§5.27), only for the items that name such a note and never over pending edits; a withheld report published as a notice that clears no Refresh asked and sets no `@git_body`; a withheld Brief or Decision log section as a notice; a page already in Notion is rewritten in place at the next render and restored once the note is clean or the report refreshed, Notion's history kept), §5.18 (a refresh rebuilds a withheld report), capture-and-review §5.1's Derived views, Reports and Indexes rows. Code in `src/lib/suppressions.ts` (`withheldView` and `viewDependencies` replace `eligibleView`; `publishableNote` gives the awaiting-cleanup form; `publishedTitle`; `eligibleNotes` gives views the same form), `src/commands/query.ts`, `src/lib/query.ts`, `src/lib/index-builder.ts`, `src/commands/index.ts`, `src/lib/views.ts`, `src/lib/views-write.ts`, `src/lib/sync.ts`, `src/lib/notion-change.ts`, `src/lib/review-body.ts`. Tests: `tests/derived-content.test.ts` (six tests: the acceptance items in order, plus the JSON index, the views' label lines, a Notion render of a note with a restricted part, its Review row and a withheld report, and a Review page's item sections on create and on a published page, held back whole while it has pending edits), and the three `cause`/`rebuild` updates in `tests/report-excluded.test.ts`.
**Spec questions:** (1) Settled by the coordinator, 2026-11-11, from eligibility.md §1 and §6 rule 5 (Review pages are a published surface): a Review page's item sections follow the rule here, changing only the items that name such a note, never over pending edits (capture-and-review §7.10); built and tested. (2) CC-176's question 4: answered (b) by the user on 2026-11-11, built here. On every answer surface (`query --read` and `--candidates`, `recall`, `reflect --candidates`) and every published surface (Notion page bodies, what a Review page shows of a note; `index.md` and the views carry no note bodies), a relative link in an eligible line to a note in the awaiting-cleanup form or restricted whole keeps its text and loses its target (cli.md §5.13 "A link to such a note", §5.13.1, §5.14.4, §5.27's `title_withheld`). This extends eligibility.md §6 rule 3 by the user's choice; eligibility.md is not edited. A proposal's own wording is not rewritten (eligibility.md §9.4, §9.5). Code: `withoutWithheldLinks` and `Context.withheldPaths` in `src/lib/suppressions.ts`, applied by `answerNote` and `publishableNote` and in `src/lib/review-body.ts`; test: the seventh test of `tests/derived-content.test.ts`. (3) Choices made under eligibility.md §6 rule 5 and §8, shown for the user: a view line that names a note by type and id is not a dependency, so a regenerated Brief comes back before the cleanup; a summary-only restriction keeps the title (rule 3, last sentence), and a view that links such a note stays withheld until the cleanup; sources keep their titles in the JSON index (the input's own name); published surfaces judge a memory a view rests on without a caller (reviewed, superseded by nothing).

### CC-184 · One upgrade brings the brain and every linked project to the corrected instructions and skills (0077 §4) · `done` (#297)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** `brain upgrade` updates your brain's instructions and skills and every linked project's copies in one step, keeps what each project wrote itself, and lists any project it could not finish; start fresh agent sessions afterwards.
**Output:** spec/eligibility.md's upgrade rule and the cli.md sections for `upgrade` and `link` first, then: one upgrade that refreshes the brain's managed instructions and skills and each registered linked project's managed block and skill copies, keeping project-owned text; a check that Claude Code's and Codex's definition paths each hold the corrected definition; a report listing inaccessible projects, duplicate or conflicting copies and missing or damaged managed blocks as unfinished, never upgraded; the closing instruction to start fresh sessions. A synthetic old installation made with the published 0.12.0 package. Files: `src/commands/upgrade.ts`, the managed-block code it shares with `src/commands/link.ts`, a new test file and its fixture or fixture script. The user's real brain is not touched.
**Acceptance:** on a synthetic 0.12.0 installation with linked projects (one plain, one with project-owned text around its block, one unreachable, one with a damaged block, one with two conflicting copies): the brain and the first two are upgraded with project-owned text byte for byte the same; the other three are reported unfinished and not counted upgraded; a second run changes nothing more; with the snapshot CC-180 vendors, both agents' definition paths hold the corrected definitions; the output asks for fresh sessions; nothing outside the synthetic folders changes; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.
**Phase 1 (built, no `src/` change):** `tests/upgrade-0-12-fixture.ts` makes the 0.12.0 installation with the published package (`npm pack ultimate-brain@0.12.0`, checked against npm's integrity, cached in the OS temp folder, run with this repo's `node_modules`; skipped when offline with no cached copy): a brain, one area, one project, and seven folders linked by 0.12.0's `brain link`, six of them registered with `brain repo add`: `plain`, `owned` (text before and after the block in AGENTS.md and in a CLAUDE.md without `@AGENTS.md`), `unreachable` (folder gone), `damaged` (no end marker), `conflicting-skill` (a second `brain-query` definition in `.claude/skills/` only), `conflicting-block` (the block twice), and `unregistered` (linked, no `repos` entry). Every run passes `--brain` and a temp `HOME` with its own Git identity. `tests/upgrade-linked.test.ts` checks the fixture and that the product checkout is unchanged, and holds the acceptance as `todo` (`CC-184 phase 2`), the definition-path check waiting for CC-180's snapshot.
**Phase 1 notes (the plan for phase 2, after CC-182 and CC-176):**
- **Files.** `src/lib/managed-block.ts` takes from `link.ts`, as a pure refactor, the block (`instructionBlock`, `blockRange`, `replaceOrAppend`), the skill source and plan (`linkSource`, `planSkills`) and the rollback write (`writePlan`), behind one `planLink(project, brain, options)`; `link` calls it with its options, `upgrade` with none, so a project keeps its `.brain` placement.
- **Finding a project.** Each `brain.json` `repos` entry (path expanded as `repo` commands do) whose root holds a `.brain`; an entry without `.brain` is a read-only source, not a linked project. `brain link` records no path (§5.12), so a folder linked but never `repo add`ed is outside the upgrade (decided below).
- **Judging it, before any write there.** `unreachable`: the path is missing, not a folder or unreadable, or `.brain` is invalid. `block_missing`: AGENTS.md, or a CLAUDE.md that doesn't import it, has no markers. `block_damaged`: unpaired or out-of-order markers. `conflicting_copies`: a block twice in one file, a `duplicate` competing copy (skills-delivery §5.2) in either skill tree, or a CLAUDE.md symlink to another file. Link's refusals (`OUTSIDE_ROOT`, `INPUT_INVALID`, `INSTRUCTIONS_SYMLINK`) become these states instead of stopping the run. An unfinished project is left exactly as it was (fail closed), never counted upgraded. Global copies stay a `SKILLS_COMPETING` warning.
- **Order.** The brain's checks and writes first (its new pin is the projects' source), then each project as one rollback-safe write; a brain refusal stops before any project write. Then the definition check: in the brain and in each upgraded project, every linked skill file in `.agents/skills/` (Codex) and `.claude/skills/` (Claude Code) equals the pin, and each instruction file holds the current block; a difference makes the project `unfinished` (`definitions_differ`). Projects are not committed (`link` never commits); the report says to commit there.
- **Report.** `data.projects`: `[{ name, path, state: upgraded | unchanged | unfinished, reason?, files: [{ path, action }] }]` in `repos` order, with one `PROJECT_UNFINISHED` warning per unfinished project; `--dry-run` plans the same. The human output ends: start fresh agent sessions in the brain and each upgraded project, since a session opened before the upgrade keeps the old instructions. The exit code with unfinished projects follows CC-182.
- **Decided by the user, 2026-11-10:** only `brain.json` `repos` entries count (spec/eligibility.md §11 step 3); a folder linked with `brain link` alone isn't tracked, and the upgrade's output ends with a line saying to register it or run `brain link` there. The fixture's `unregistered` folder covers it: a todo test asserts the line and that the folder is untouched.
**Built (phase 2):** cli.md §5.15 step 9 (linked projects: source, the judging table, an entry with no `.brain`, both agents' check, states, report, exit code, human output), §5.12's pointer to it, eligibility.md §13 row 12. `src/lib/managed-block.ts`: link's block, `.brain` and skill planning moved out of `link.ts` unchanged (`planLinkedRepo`, `applyLinkedRepo`). `src/lib/upgrade-projects.ts`: `planProjects` judges and plans every `repos` entry before the brain's first write (keeping `.brain`'s `repo` name and placement), `writeProjects` writes each project with rollback and checks both trees and both instruction files, `skillsDiffer` checks the brain's trees too; `upgrade.ts` only calls them and adds `data.projects`, `data.next`, `PROJECT_UNFINISHED`, `DEFINITIONS_DIFFER` and exit 1, so CC-190's `notion_migrations` stays apart. `tests/upgrade-linked.test.ts`: the acceptance on the 0.12.0 installation (dry run, upgraded and unfinished projects, second run, fresh-sessions line, nothing outside, the unregistered folder), and step 9's other cases on the fixture brain (`block_missing`, a CLAUDE.md block that differs, a malformed `.brain`, a global copy, `not_linked`, `write_failed` with its rollback, a kept repo name and a CLAUDE.md symlink, a brain with no projects, the definition check).
**After CC-180 (#301):** the test "with the snapshot CC-180 vendors, Claude Code's and Codex's definition paths hold the corrected definitions" is real: a 0.12.0 installation upgraded by this CLI is pinned to the vendored brain-v1.6.0, and every snapshot file, in both trees, of the brain and of each upgraded project holds the vendored bytes (brain-query among them, changed since 1.5.0).
**Spec questions (answered by the user on 2026-11-11, each as built):** (1) A `repos` entry whose folder has no `.brain` and no managed-block marker (a read-only source the user never ran `brain link` in) is reported `not_linked`, nothing written, neither upgraded nor unfinished; eligibility.md §11 counts every entry with a path, which would report such a source `block_missing` on every run. (2) A global copy of a linked skill (`~/.claude/skills/brain-query`) makes every project `unfinished` (`conflicting_copies`), reading §11 step 3 literally; it stays a warning for the brain. (3) Unfinished projects make the exit code 1 (cli.md §3.4, completed with findings); the brain's writes and commit stand. The user's answers: (1) `not_linked`, neither upgraded nor unfinished; (2) every project `unfinished` (`conflicting_copies`) per 0077 §4, the report naming the file to remove; (3) exit 1, the brain's writes standing.

### CC-199 · task.mjs start --resume continues an in-progress task on a new branch · `done` (#292)
**Release:** 0.13.0

**Output:** `scripts/task.mjs`: `start <id> --resume` (a bare flag, not with `--new`) accepts only a row that is `in progress` on the base and refuses while an unmerged remote branch of the task is open; a merged branch of the same name is history, so the new branch may reuse it. **Acceptance:** `tests/release-scripts.test.mjs`, "task start --resume continues an in-progress task whose branch merged, and only that (CC-199)": a merged remote branch of the same name doesn't block; the worktree has the branch, the hooks and an unchanged row; a second resume, a `todo` or `review` row and a task with an unmerged open branch are refused; `--resume` with `--new`, twice, or with `done` is a usage error; plain `start` on an `in progress` row names `--resume`. `npm run test:scripts`, the leak and PR format checks pass. First built as #290 under the id CC-195, which #289 had taken on its branch; renumbered.
