### CC-203 · The English retrieval evaluation: eighty audited cases, two scoring levels, the cost tool and a baseline (0080) · `done` (#310)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** development only: eighty audited English retrieval cases (facts, paraphrases, unresolved subjects, unknowns) are scored separately as first-search candidates and as final answers, with latency and output tokens measured against a matched baseline.
**Output:** `eval/retrieval-0.13/` (new), following spec/retrieval.md §5 to §7: a generator that builds `eval/search-0.5`'s original brain unchanged and adds the retrieval layer and the caller folders; `cases.json` (generated) with 20 cases per group, each with its `evidence_sets` or `subjects`, `facts`, `as_of`, `split` and `audit`; the audit check (§5.5); a scorer with the retrieval level (one first search, default limit, question verbatim) and the answer level (memory-0.12's answer rules, `forbidden_facts` on every kind, and CC-201's `abstains` with its facts-first rule, imported from `eval/memory-0.12/score.mjs`; plus `clarify`); a reference-answers adapter; the cost tool (§7: paired, interleaved latency, cold CLI and in-process ranking; o200k tokens with the pinned measurement-only `gpt-tokenizer` 4.1.0; full-read cost reported); a README; a provisional baseline on this task's base, labelled provisional. `eval/search-0.5` unchanged.
**Built:** `eval/retrieval-0.13/` (`world.mjs`, `cases.mjs`, `make-brain.mjs`, `cases.json`, `reference-answers.json`, `lib.mjs`, `score.mjs`, `adapters/`, `originals.mjs`, `costs.mjs`, `README.md`, `results-provisional.txt`) and `tests/retrieval-eval.test.mjs`. The layer has 12 projects with similar names and, per spec/retrieval.md §5.1 as amended in CC-202, the original brain's note density (companion notes from a seeded stream that hold none of the cases' facts). **Finding:** on a sparse layer (4 to 6 notes per subject) today's search found 20 of 20 facts and 19 of 20 paraphrases, because all of a subject's notes fit in the first ten; with the original density, at this head (the trust-safe search merged), it finds **6 of 17 facts in prose only** (the original fact-in-body 0 of 10, the new body-only facts 6 of 7: the user's 16 of 17 gate is not met), 19 of 20 facts in all (body-only 6 of 7, structured 12 of 12, decision 1 of 1; 17 of 20 by prose alone), 15 of 20 paraphrases (not met), and two subjects visible in 14 of 20 clarify cases. The originals stay 28/32/36, MRR 0.61. The cases and their subgroups were frozen on 2026-11-11 before any comparison, with the 80 questions and every evidence alternative unchanged.
**Acceptance:** the scorer reports the prose-only fact gate (16 of 17: the original 10 fact-in-body questions and the 7 `body-only` facts; spec/retrieval.md §6.2) before the combined fact gate, and the facts by frozen subgroup (body-only 7, structured 12, decision 1, which the audit checks) and by prose alone; the generator is deterministic (two runs, identical bytes) and its brain lints with no errors; `eval/search-0.5/questions.json` and the original brain's notes are byte for byte the same; the audit passes and fails on a planted bad case of each kind it checks; the reference answers pass every answer rule; the scorer runs end to end on today's `brain` (passing is not required); the 50 originals rerun with the ranks of `results-cc173.txt`; no case names a real person, client or brain; `npm test`, `npm run test:scripts`, the leak and PR format checks pass.

**Release:** 0.13.0

### CC-200 · A native-run harness for the final acceptance: Claude Code and Codex sessions with traces, token accounting and the no-file-read check · `done` (#303)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** development only: a harness runs every memory-evaluation case as a fresh native Claude Code or Codex session on a packaged CLI, keeps each session's trace and token use, and checks that no session read a brain file for an answer.
**Output:** `eval/memory-0.12/native/` only (and its test in `tests/`): `run.mjs` builds the synthetic brain with `make-brain.mjs` in a sandbox whose `HOME` is a temporary folder, installs the given package there, links the caller repos with that package's `brain link`, and runs each case as a fresh non-interactive session in its caller repo (`claude -p --output-format stream-json`, `codex exec --json`), each on its own copy of the brain, with a timeout, a failing `gh` stub, no MCP servers and the agent's default model; the case that follows an upgrade runs `brain upgrade` first. Per session it keeps the full trace, the reply, the agent's own token usage and the brain output delivered (opening and later, o200k tokens), and classifies every tool call: a brain CLI call (answer surface, administrative or write, spec/eligibility.md §7, §10) or a read, search or listing of a path under the brain folder, which fails the session (§12 item 4). The answers, citations, full reads and proposal or upgrade outcomes go to the unchanged `score.mjs` through `native/adapter.mjs`; `results.mjs` writes the results file CC-186 commits: the identity header (package file and sha256, CLI version, commit, snapshot name, version and integrity, generator commit and output hash), per-agent gates with the read check and token accounting, and every failure with its cause. No case, scoring rule or product file changes.
**Acceptance:** `tests/native-run.test.mjs` covers, with no agent: the classifier (Read, Grep, Glob and shell reads, searches and listings under the brain flagged, including `cd` into it, `~`, `$BRAIN_DIR` and relative paths; brain CLI calls, reads of the caller repo and of the agent's own skills not flagged), both agents' trace parsing and token accounting on synthetic traces, citation extraction (ids, note paths, task rows), and the identity header. A smoke run of 3 cases (one critical trust, one answerable, one unknown) per agent on a package packed from this branch completes end to end; its figures, time and tokens are in the PR body, not committed as acceptance. `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, the leak and PR format checks pass.
**Method choices: the user's of 2026-11-11** (accepted as proposed in #303): (1) each agent logs in from a dedicated folder (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`), never `~/.claude` or `~/.codex`, inside a temporary HOME that on macOS links only `Library/Keychains` for Claude Code's login; (2) the prompt is the case's question, after "I'm working on <task> in this repo." when the case names a task, then one fixed line asking for `Evidence: <ids>`, whose ids alone are the citations; (3) every case runs on its own fresh copy of the world; (4) Claude Code gets Read, Glob, Grep, Skill and sandboxed Bash, with Edit, Write, sub-agents and the web off; (5) a read of `brain.json` or the brain's product files (`.agents/`, `.claude/`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`) is recorded, not a violation. The scorer's abstain pattern (wording misses in the smoke run) is CC-201's, not this task's.
**After the review (#303):** the check also flags a brain path written inside a longer word: an interpreter's inline script (`python3 -c`, `node -e`), a here-document's body, and a `$(…)` or backticks whose output feeds another command; a path an interpreter builds at run time is the README's stated limit (`tests/native-run.test.mjs`, "the classifier flags a brain path inside an inline script, a here-document or a substitution").

### CC-201 · The memory evaluation's scorer recognises an honest unknown answer by its meaning, not a loose phrase · `done` (#304)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** development only: the memory evaluation's scorer recognises an honest "I couldn't find it" as an abstention, and no longer takes an answer that mentions "no recorded decision" beside its facts for one.
**Output:** `eval/memory-0.12/score.mjs`: the `ABSTAIN` pattern becomes a two-kind rule over the answer's clauses. A phrase saying the speaker or the brain lacks the answer (`SAYS_UNKNOWN`: couldn't/can't/didn't find, not in the brain, no record or information, not recorded, I don't know…) abstains anywhere; a phrase saying a thing is absent (`SAYS_ABSENT`: "no recorded decision", "no … is recorded") abstains only when every clause holds one of the two or is `NEUTRAL` (the Evidence line, search narration). On an answerable case the facts decide (the user's choice, 2026-11-11, from the review of #304): an answer holding every required fact passes whatever caveat it adds; an abstaining answer with a fact missing, or on a case with no facts, fails as abstained. Trust and unknown cases count the abstention anywhere (trust-12 unchanged); a guessed forbidden fact fails through `forbidden_facts`. The script's body moves into `main()`, run only as a script, so tests import `abstains` and `scoreAnswer`. The README's scoring section states the rule ("Abstention"). Found by CC-200's smoke run (#303): two correct "I couldn't find" abstentions scored wrong, and an answer counted as an abstention for "has no recorded decision". The 108 cases and the reference answers are unchanged.
**Acceptance:** `tests/memory-eval-score.test.mjs`: a table of 30 phrasings (the smoke run's 3, 17 more abstentions, 10 more answers) read as the README says; `abstained: true` counts; the smoke run's unknown passes and its answer doesn't abstain; facts first: the Harbor caveat example passes an answerable case, an abstaining answer with a fact missing or on a case with no facts fails, trust-12's reference answer abstains and passes, a guess beside a caveat fails a trust case through `forbidden_facts`; every reference answer abstains exactly where its case asks. `node eval/memory-0.12/score.mjs <out> --adapter reference-answers` passes 108/108 before and after; `cases.json` has the same sha256. `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts` and the leak check pass.

### CC-218 · A rejected draft decision never enters a decision log as accepted, and a replayed plan never resets a drafted revision · `done` (#317)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** a draft decision you reject in Review no longer appears in the project's decision log as accepted, and applying an old plan again no longer resets a revision the brain already drafted.
**Output:** from CC-215's synthetic runs (#315). (1) spec/schema.md §16.2: an `archived` decision that is not `superseded` (a draft rejected in Review or closed on excluded evidence, or one you archived) is in neither section of a decision log; spec/eligibility.md §8 gains the rule that a derived item never shows an archived note as current; `src/lib/views.ts` follows. (2) spec/cli.md §5.27: a `review_revisions` entry from a plan whose `as_of` is not later than the `drafted_at` of the item's drafted revision is skipped `STALE_PLAN`, so replaying a plan is a no-op; `src/lib/review-revision.ts` follows. (3) `eval/memory-0.12/README.md` and `eval/retrieval-0.13/README.md` point at decision 0082 for when the complete native run happens. Tests in `tests/views.test.ts` and `tests/review-apply.test.ts`.
**Acceptance:** on a synthetic brain, a draft decision rejected through a Review submission is not in the project's `decision-log.md` after `brain views` (neither section, nor `generated_from`), while a superseded decision stays under Superseded and a draft still in Review stays listed (0041's history); applying the same plan again after `proposal revise` drafted the revision leaves the revision `drafted` with its `previous` and reports `skipped` `STALE_PLAN`, and a new submission planned after the draft is still recorded; the spec states both rules before the code; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.

### CC-214 · The Review summary names each item's outcome by its letter and title, and where a deferred item went · `done` (#312)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** the summary at the top of a Review page now lists each item by its letter and title with what happened to it (applied, rejected, closed, deferred with a link to the row it moved to, a revision on its way, or waiting for you), so you no longer work out from counts which items still need you; the first sync after upgrading updates the summary of each Review row still in Notion once.
**Output:** the per-item summary specified in `spec/capture-and-review.md` §7.4 (a) and the Review page paragraph of `spec/cli.md` §5.14.4, then built in `src/lib/review-body.ts` (with the row's Name rule passed in from `src/lib/sync.ts`) and covered by a focused test file with synthetic fixtures. The count line stays; one line per item follows it, in the row's item order. Each outcome comes from the proposal file's canonical state (`item_decisions`, `revisions`, a whole-row decision's `exclude` and `dropped`) and is matched by `item_id`, never by letter. A deferred item names the row it now waits in, following later deferrals, and links its page only once that row is published. Titles are the ones the item's section heading uses, so a restricted title stays withheld. Item sections and their write rules are unchanged. Not in scope: the applied wording or values in the summary, the skills fork (the guide's example count line stays true), and the other three items of the 2026-11-11 review.
**Acceptance:** one mixed synthetic row has an applied item (with the user's edit), a rejected one, a deferred one, one with a requested revision and an undecided one, and its summary names each by letter and title with that outcome under the count line; once the revision is drafted, that item reads as revised wording waiting for a decision. An item closed because it rests on excluded text reads as closed for that reason, not as rejected. A row decided as a whole (0045) with a deferred, a stale and a withheld item is described item by item, and its counts match. A deferred item's line names the destination row and says it awaits publication, with no link, before `sync record` gives that row a page; it links that page afterwards; after a second deferral, it names the last row in the chain. Outcomes follow item ids: `item_decisions` stored in a different order from `items` doesn't move them, and the destination row lists the moved item under its new letter. An item whose note is in the awaiting-cleanup form is named `<type> <id>, title withheld: awaiting cleanup`, as its section heading names it. A render of an unchanged row sends no update. An update never sends a body and lists no section of an applied, closed or deferred item. `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.
**Choices within the handoff:** (1) the summary names each item's outcome by its letter and title but shows no wording or value (the handoff's illustrative "Applied: 18 March" is not followed): it answers which items still need the user with nothing to leak; agreed with the coordinator on 2026-11-11. (2) A pending held item reads "Held: … Reject or Defer it.", not "Waiting for your decision.", since it cannot be accepted.
Moved to 0.13.0 by the user, 2026-11-11: merged early (#312), so it ships in 0.13.0; the first sync after the upgrade rewrites each live Review page's summary once (its items untouched).

### CC-223 · The memory evaluation's scorer doesn't count a restated or denied question as stating a forbidden fact · `done` (#323)
**Release:** 0.13.0
**Changelog:** development only: the memory evaluation's scorer no longer counts an answer that restates or denies the question ("nothing says whether the dispatchers prefer SMS") as stating the forbidden fact, and reads "I couldn't establish" as an honest unknown.
**Output:** `eval/memory-0.12/score.mjs`: a forbidden fact counts only where the answer states it. A pattern's match is not a statement when both hold: its text only repeats the case's question (it appears in the question, lower case, curly apostrophes straight), and its sentence asks or restates the question (ends with `?`, or `whether`/`if` comes before the match in the same clause) or denies it (`nothing … says/records/holds…`, `no note/record/memory/evidence/session…`, `not/n't … say/record/hold/confirm…` before the match in the same clause), or the match is inside a quoted search query (`searches for "…"`, only other quoted terms between the verb and it). Everything else stays a statement: a caveat beside a stated fact ("Dispatchers prefer SMS, but this isn't approved"), "No, dispatchers prefer SMS", a match spanning two sentences, and any figure or wording the question doesn't hold, even inside a denial ("Nothing records a 2,000 penalty" still fails). `SAYS_UNKNOWN` gains "couldn't/could not establish, confirm or determine" (curly apostrophes are already read as straight). The README's scoring section states the rule ("Forbidden facts"). Found by 0.13.0's native smoke run at 251ff77 (decision 0082): trust-01 (both agents) and trust-34 (Claude Code) failed on a restated, denied or quoted-as-searched question, and Codex's trust-01 abstention ("I couldn’t establish whether …") wasn't recognised. trust-39 (Claude Code) and trust-29 (Codex) are not the scorer's and stay as they are. The 108 cases and the reference answers are unchanged.

## 0.14.0 — Repeatable onboarding and early access (decisions 0061, 0078, 0079, 0081)

**Moved from 0.15.0 by [0078](../decisions/0078-onboarding-and-early-access-in-0-14-0.md)** (the user's grill of 2026-11-10): onboarding comes before the weekly review so testers start earlier; the trust repair stays 0.13.0 and first (0077); **early access for friends and family starts on 0.14.0**. A new user duplicates a **published Notion template** (since [0084](../decisions/0084-the-notion-template-is-the-authors-emptied-space-with-example-rows.md): a copy of the author's own space, emptied and scrubbed, with seven `Example · ` rows that setup removes when it connects), installs the CLI, runs `brain init` and says **"set up my brain"**: one guided, resumable setup (prerequisites, one settings screen with defaults and a one-line explanation each, the template and the Notion connection, connect, identities, seeding through one preview, the first-value check, the schedule offered last). First value is correct tasks, a report and the information organised, proven by a release gate in a clean duplicated workspace and by each user's setup. Notion AI is required for early access and becomes optional at 1.0 with what exists. 0061's minimal per-area tracking preferences stay in this release's scope as recorded; their contract is CC-188's. Out of scope: an interactive `brain init`, a new diagnostic command, telemetry, anything in the brain made from an Example row, rich profiles (0030), the template gallery and the funnel (1.0), and any operation on the user's brain or Notion workspace without an explicit yes.

**Response style and plain writing ([0081](../decisions/0081-response-style-working-principles-and-plain-writing.md), the user's grill of 2026-11-11).** Each user chooses how the brain talks to them: `settings.style` with three dials (explaining, length, directness) and two presets, Standard (today, the default) and Simple; offered on the settings screen and changeable in chat. The CLI writes it as a paragraph in the brain's `AGENTS.md` and in linked folders' blocks; it shapes the agent's own words only, never CLI-rendered reports. The brain's rules gain four working principles restated from the Karpathy guidelines and three reply rules for everyone. Humanizer is copied unchanged into the skills fork as a general skill, and a short writing reference inside `brain/` guides the text operational skills write for people.

**Lean minutes ([0079](../decisions/0079-lean-minutes-and-follow-ups-as-tasks.md), the user's grill of 2026-11-10).** The minutes get a readable part (one line, Quick follow-ups, Tasks for the brain, one lean block per subject with prefixed lines and id tags) and a collapsed For the brain toggle (Attendees, Projects, Selection lines, the id → quote list); each item is written once. A quick follow-up still unticked at the Reviewed read becomes a Task (no due date, a `q` id so it is never created twice; the checkbox is left alone). The brain still stores the selected transcript paragraphs; older minutes and the voice format are read as before. It ships here so testers start on it; a T6-style run of the prompt gates it.

**No pull requests in a brain, and `gh` for early access ([0086](../decisions/0086-the-brain-works-on-main-and-early-access-requires-gh.md), the user's conversation of 2026-11-12).** Operating a brain never needs a pull request or a merge on GitHub: every write is committed on the brain's working branch and pushed, the brain's rules say so against agents' code-repository habits, and `brain status` warns when the brain is off that branch, which an attended session fixes locally. Early access requires a GitHub account and `gh` signed in: setup creates the private remote with it, and the steps for another Git host return at 1.0. Testers read "backup" instead of Git words, and "fix my backup" repairs it. Out of scope: this repository's own pull requests (0040, 0073), other Git hosts for early access, a brain on two machines (`docs/v2-ideas.md`).

Work packages (0061, kept): onboarding/diagnostic/preference contracts; probe-first setup and selected seeding reusing 0.9.0 project adoption and the 0.10.0 assistant as the guide (0064); common-failure recovery guidance; minimal per-area tracking preferences; fresh-user first capture, review and cited answer. Rich profiles and specialised types remain deferred.

**Plan (CC-187, 2026-11-10).** Contracts first; every task conforms to CC-188's `spec/setup.md`. Nothing here starts before 0.13.0's tasks leave room: the trust repair ships first. A task edits only what it owns plus its own row; skill changes go through the skills fork and are vendored with `scripts/vendor-skills.mjs`.

### CC-191 · The `brain-setup` skill: one guided, resumable setup with seeding and the first-value check (0078 §2, §3, §5) · `done` (#302)
**Release:** 0.14.0
**Changelog:** say "set up my brain" and your agent walks you through everything once: the settings, the Notion connection, your work's Domains, Areas, Projects, people and key documents, and a first check that tasks, a report and your notes came out right.
**Output:** in the skills fork, through fork PRs, released in brain-v1.7.0 and vendored by CC-219 (#316): `brain/brain-setup/` (new) per `spec/setup.md`, ordering the steps and resuming from `brain status`'s setup block plus its online checks; the settings screen; the two Notion steps walked through and checked; seeding through Ultimate Assistant's "tell it what's on your mind" flow extended to Domains, Areas and People (`brain/ultimate-assistant/`), with project adoption for a folder or repo; the first-value check and the setup report. `brain/brain-connect-notion/` stores the template version and no longer asks for modules a template already holds beyond on/off. The brain's `AGENTS.md` operations table gains the setup row. The path without Notion AI is built here and documented as 1.0's. Per CC-213: step 7 per CC-213 (setup.md §3, §10, §12): four checks, with the cited answer (one document-derived item accepted in Review and applied, one useful question, the answer and its passage confirmed by the user); one first-value line per attempt with each check's outcome, result=done only when every applicable check passes, else result=failed reason=first-value-unresolved; unresolved stops at step 7 with what failed, the smallest next action and how to resume, with no schedule offer and no finished line; resume runs only the checks not passed (all four after newer seeding, tasks when Tasks is turned on); the setup report's step 7 line and "not offered" for step 8; the first-meeting line (§10.1) only after a passing first-value line.
**Acceptance:** a synthetic fresh brain driven through the skill reaches every step in order, resumes at the first unfinished one after an interruption, and writes nothing in seeding before the yes; the fork's checks pass; the brain-setup skill reaches `.agents/skills` through brain-v1.7.0, vendored by CC-219 (#316), the user's choice of 2026-11-11, so this PR merges after #316; `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass. Per CC-213: in a synthetic run: a failed check leaves first value unresolved and resumable, and the schedule is not offered; on resume, a passed check whose material is unchanged is not run again; with Tasks off, tasks shows "skipped (Tasks off)"; a seeded document with no commitment of the owner, or no Review item, is unsuitable, with no invented task, item or answer; after the user's correction, resume reaches result=done and then offers the schedule; the setup report shown while unresolved writes no finished line; a catch-up while unresolved shows no first-meeting line.
**After CC-213:** step 7 follows CC-213's correction of spec/setup.md §2, §3, §10 and §12; the fork's brain-setup changes once CC-213 is merged.
**Built so far (phase 1, fork PR and this PR as drafts; 0.14.0 merges after 0.13.0 ships):** the fork's `brain/brain-setup/` (SKILL.md: the rules, the `setup` log line, start or resume with the online checks, the step order, the path without Notion AI; references `prerequisites.md`, `settings.md` (the screen, applying it, the `AGENTS.md` lines), `notion.md` (steps 3, 4, 4a with its synthetic sample meeting, 4b, 5), `seeding.md`, `first-value.md` (steps 7 and 8), `report.md` (fields, filing after a yes, reason codes)); `brain/brain-connect-notion/` (step 4a stores `notion.template_version` from the System page's line, unknown when absent; step 0 is skipped in guided setup; a template duplicate's modules are on or off only); the fork's range `>=0.14.0-0 <0.15.0`; here, `templates/brain/AGENTS.md`'s operations row for `brain-setup` and the spec fix below. Evidence: three blind runs by fresh sub-agents on a synthetic `brain init` brain with CC-190's CLI (#296 head `d0c52cf`, built from a scratch clone), a failing `gh` stub, an isolated HOME and Git config, and a file-based Notion stand-in (no real Notion): run 1 went through steps 1 and 2 and stopped at step 3 (4 of 9); run 2 resumed with steps 3 to 5 recorded but no Notion tools, reopened step 3 and stopped there; run 3 resumed through step 3 to seeding, stopped at the preview with nothing written (one log line only), then after "yes to all" wrote 16 notes (People `draft`), and ran first value, the schedule (declined), the report (not sent) and `finished`. The fixes each run found are in the fork PR. After CC-213 (#314), step 7 was rebuilt and checked by two more runs from run 3's seeded brain (CC-190's CLI `d0c52cf`, the same stand-ins): run 4, Tasks on, with a seeded document holding no commitment of the owner and its Review row rejected, logged `result=failed reason=first-value-unresolved tasks=unsuitable report=pass organised=fail answer=unsuitable`, created no task, item or answer, offered no schedule, and showed the setup report (step 8 `not offered`) with no `finished` line; run 5, after the user turned Tasks off and dropped a second document, kept `report`, showed `tasks` as `skipped (Tasks off)`, ran `organised` and `answer` again (one item accepted, a question answered with its passage), logged `result=done`, then offered the schedule (declined) and wrote `finished`. Not built yet: a catch-up shows no first-meeting line while first value is unresolved (brain-catch-up, phase 2).
**Phase 2 (built, after CC-180's fork PR merged):**
- Ultimate Assistant's ramble places new Domains, Areas (with `track`), Orgs and people (`draft`) and hands a folder or repo to brain-workflow-adopt; its "Seeding" section is the flow setup's step 6 runs (assistant.md §7; setup.md §8).
- brain-ingest's "What an Area tracks" uses an area's `track` (setup.md §11).
- brain-catch-up's first-meeting line is said once, only after a passing first-value line (§10.1).
- brain-notion-change raises `notion.template_version` after a migration and leaves an unknown version unknown (§9).

Evidence:
- **Run 6** (pre-seeding brain, Notion stand-in): seeding went through the ramble's Seeding section. The preview was changed once, then "yes to all". It wrote 16 notes (People `draft`, Clients' `track` in the user's words), published them, and set the Projects' Status and the Domains' Type. It took in one document, whose ingest kept the bathroom budget as a fact because Clients tracks budgets.

**Output:** a bounded correction of CC-188's contract, which stays as recorded. `spec/setup.md`: §2 (step 7 is done only when first value passes; step 8 is offered only then), §3 (resume retries the checks not passed and keeps those passed, unless seeding ran again or the Tasks module changed), §4.1 (one `first-value` line per attempt: `result=done` only when every applicable check passes, else `result=failed reason=first-value-unresolved`; each check `pass`, `fail`, `unsuitable` or `skip`, a check not yet run left out; the new `answer` key), §5 (when the recorded `first-value` counts), §10 (an attempt is not a pass; the fourth check, one document-derived item accepted in Review and a useful question answered with its supporting passage, which the user confirms; unsuitable material is unresolved and nothing is invented; what the user is shown; the setup report doesn't finish setup; §10.1, the first meeting counts only after a passing `first-value` line), §12 (the report's first-value line and the `first-value-unresolved` code). `spec/cli.md` §5.2: `recorded.first-value` set only by a passing line, so an earlier `result=done` line with a failed or missing check is unresolved; the new `first_value` key with each check's latest outcome. Planning lines only: CC-193's Acceptance gains the handoff's additions, and the PR body lists what CC-191 (#302) adds to its own row and what a follow-up to CC-190 builds, since CC-190 (#296) merged before this contract: until that follow-up, `status` on `main` still reads CC-188's form. No code, skill or Notion change.
**Acceptance:** §10 says an attempt is recorded and first value passes only when every applicable check passes, where a Tasks-off `skip` is shown as a skip, never as a pass, and makes the tasks check not applicable only while Tasks is off; a `fail`, an `unsuitable` check or a check not yet run leaves first value unresolved, keeps `next` at `first-value` and withholds the schedule offer; resume re-runs only the checks not passed unless seeding ran again or the Tasks module changed; an unsuitable input is recorded as `unsuitable` with a reason and the smallest next action, never a made-up task, item or answer; the cited-answer check names the accepted item, the question, the answer and its supporting passage; cli.md §5.2 reads a `result=done` line with a `fail` or without all four checks as unresolved; producing the setup report writes no `finished` line and unlocks nothing; CC-193's row carries the fresh-user record of the reviewed item, question, answer and passage; CC-188's row is unchanged; only spec Markdown and planning rows change; the leak and PR format checks pass.
**Spec questions:** none open. The source is the usability review the user approved on 2026-11-11 (its first item); calls made within it, for the review: (1) the cited-answer check is a fourth key, `answer`, beside 0078 §5's three, which it adds to and replaces none; it is 0061's "fresh-user first capture, review and cited answer" work package, which the 0.14.0 section already lists; (2) unresolved reuses `result=failed` with one code, `first-value-unresolved`, since the check keys already say what is wrong, so `status`'s reading of `failed` stays and only a passing-line rule is added; (3) a check not run in an attempt is left out of the line instead of a new value, so a line written under CC-188's contract (no `answer`) reads as unresolved, never as success; (4) `status` gains `first_value` with the latest line's timestamp, so the skill resumes from the setup block as for every other step; (5) a kept pass runs again only after new seeding or with Tasks turned on: a changed document can't be told apart without recording its id, which §4.1 forbids.

### CC-230 · the release scripts' pre-commit hook test no longer fails at random while cleaning up (ENOTEMPTY) · `done` (#338)
**Release:** 0.14.0
**Changelog:** development only: the release scripts' tests turn off git's background maintenance, so a temp repo is no longer removed while a detached `git maintenance` still writes into it (ENOTEMPTY).
**Output:** the cause is in the tests, not in a script: since Git 2.5x every `git commit` starts a detached `git maintenance run --auto`, which repacks the pre-commit hook test's temp repo and writes `.git/info/refs` and `.git/objects/info/packs` after the commit returned, while `rmSync` removes the folder. CC-158 turned automatic maintenance off for the CLI tests through `tests/helpers.ts`, which `tests/release-scripts.test.mjs` never imports. `disableGitAutoMaintenance` moves to a dependency-free `tests/git-env.ts`; `tests/helpers.ts` and `tests/release-scripts.test.mjs` call it, so every git the release scripts' tests start, directly or through a script, runs with `maintenance.auto=false` and `gc.auto=0`. The test's assertions and its cleanup are unchanged.
**Acceptance:** the pre-commit hook test run 30 times in a loop fails at random during cleanup before the change and passes 30 of 30 after, the counts in the PR; a commit in the release scripts' tests starts no `git maintenance run` (a test with `GIT_TRACE`); the pre-commit hook test checks what it checked before; only test files and this row change; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.

The quick wins, in brain-catch-up only: the Unattended and Run on a schedule sections move to references read only by those runs; each skill and reference is read at its step, and only when the step has work; an input's text is never printed when the command that takes it returns it.
**Acceptance:** the breakdown is in eval/cc-264/RESULTS.md and the PR with no personal content, and corrects the gate's total (the second run's cost includes the first); each quick win names the gate's numbers it reduces; the sections moved keep their headings in SKILL.md, so every reference to brain-catch-up's "Unattended" or "Run on a schedule" still resolves; the fork PR passes its checks and is reviewed with this PR; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.
**Amended by the author's choice of 2026-11-14:** no synthetic before/after runs, and no re-run of the gate on rc.3 (CC-266 dropped the same day): the cost after the quick wins is unmeasured and will show in the author's normal use. The publish in a sub-agent is CC-267 in 1.0.0, not a quick win.

## 0.15.0 — Knowledge pays back (decisions 0061, 0064, 0075; moved by 0077 and 0078)

**Moved from 0.13.0 by [0077](../decisions/0077-trust-repair-in-0-13-0.md), then to 0.15.0 by [0078](../decisions/0078-onboarding-and-early-access-in-0-14-0.md), scope unchanged; still the feature cutoff.** The opening grill ran on 2026-11-07 and is locked in [0075](../decisions/0075-weekly-review-and-targeted-corrections.md). One weekly report has What changed, Task analysis and What needs your decision, with roughly two minutes to read and five minutes to decide. Analyse meaningful changes since the last successful brief and current outstanding work; recommend an order of attention with reasons. Keep one report per week for browsable Notion history, updating that week's report on rerun under existing module/publishing rules.

Work packages: contracts and representative resource-budget trials; shared digest/assistant selection with at most five actionable decisions and five affected targets per run, selective repetition and existing dismissals; targeted checks queued after approved corrections that change meaning, with well-supported claim-grouped/selectable proposals preserving independent and historical evidence; bounded work with honest partial coverage and queued remainder; one on-demand/scheduled weekly flow, default Monday 08:00 local when enabled, separate from catch-up and applying nothing; synthetic busy/quiet/missed-week/partial/repeated/correction scenarios plus one attended private-brain trial. Quiet runs write nothing; failures and incomplete coverage are surfaced. Numeric token/time defaults come from trials before production implementation is committed. CC-111 retains its identity; Claude Code decomposes implementation after contracts. This scope record starts none.

**Added by [0088](../decisions/0088-a-review-row-says-it-needs-you-and-a-review-page-reads-plainly.md) (2026-11-13):** a Review page you can read, CC-240 and CC-241, on the user's decision in CC-239's grill. The weekly review's scope is unchanged.

**Added by [0092](../decisions/0092-move-a-note-to-another-area.md) (2026-11-13):** moving a note to another Area, CC-251, from dogfood #345 on the user's decision in CC-249's triage. The weekly review's scope is unchanged.

**Scope grill, 2026-11-13 ([0093](../decisions/0093-the-0-15-0-scope-grill.md)):** CC-111 runs beside the CC-240 → CC-241 stack, contracts and trials first; `/Weekly review` in Notion is the way into the weekly report; the weekly schedule offers Reports once; Sonnet by default for the scheduled run. No new feature joins 0.15.0.

**Release:** 0.15.0
**Changelog:** a Review page from a meeting or a document reads plainly: what it brings and what to do at the top, its summary first, then the changes, the new notes and the links under their own headings, each change's wording shown without opening a toggle, and the people linked to one note decided as one item.
**Output:** per 0088 §2, contract first (spec/capture-and-review.md §7 and §9, spec/cli.md §5.14.2 to §5.14.4, spec/schema.md §11 for a grouped link item), then `src/lib/review-body.ts`, `sync plan` and `sync apply`: the Status line and one sentence at the top; the source's own summary note first, in its toggle, still an item; groups in the order changes, new notes, links; plain headings with the note type ("New person: …", "Adds to …", "⚠ <fact> on …: old → new"); the item id in a gray `ref` line, out of the heading; no repeated title line, no filler Why, no per-item What Accept does; a summary that counts by group until items are decided; one item per note and field for links and aliases, where a name removed from the wording is not added; the wording of changes and links visible, a new note in a toggle; `^[src-…#¶n:hash]` markers shown as `[¶n]` and expanded on read; at the first publish after the upgrade, every pending row with no typed decision, no edit and no Submit rebuilt once as a new page (the old one trashed, the count reported). Starts with a round trip on a synthetic Notion page (written through the API after the user's yes, edited by the user, read back), recorded in capture-and-review §9. Rows already touched, the decision words, Submit and the apply rules stay as they are.
**Added by [0090](../decisions/0090-review-by-grill-notion-ai-reviews-with-you-and-your-call-decides.md):** the brain-context toggle (§2: passages, what each item changes, unsure flags, repeats, related pages, the rules), rebuilt by every render and never read as wording; headings derived and never read, items found by their `ref` line; `Agreed in review:` lines excluded from the hashed text and stored in `item_decisions`; a marker only from the item's listed passages, and `VALUE_NOT_IN_PASSAGE` holding a fact change whose value is in none of its cited passages (naming the passage that holds it); Your call on a row with sections (§5: Approve submits the page as it reads, Request revision with What to fix drafts revisions at the next attended run and clears Your call, `REVISION_WITHOUT_TEXT`, Reject; `EDITED_PROPOSED_NEEDS_SUBMIT` and `ROW_REVISION_NOT_SUPPORTED` retired for these rows); the deterministic unsure flags (§6: two values for one field). The round trip runs the Grill review skill on the synthetic page, not a hand edit.
**Acceptance:** the round trip passes and is recorded; tests on synthetic proposals cover each rule of the Output (the parse of visible wording, an unknown `[¶n]` label leaving the item unreadable, a grouped link item with a removed name, the rebuild selecting only untouched rows) and that an item's hashes still bind exactly what the page showed; tests for each 0090 rule (an edited heading ignored, an `Agreed in review:` line stored and not hashed, a swapped marker accepted only from the listed passages, `VALUE_NOT_IN_PASSAGE` on the live run's `9 [¶1]`, Approve on an edited page applying the edited wording, Approve leaving a ⚠ or flagged item that is still Pending, `VALUE_NOT_IN_PASSAGE` for a number, a date, a name and a status, a rejected fact change leaving the field unchanged, Request revision without text refused, Request revision drafting the revisions at an attended run and clearing Your call, an unattended run leaving a Request revision row untouched); spec and code agree; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.

## 0.15.1

Text and configuration for the friends-and-family beta, from the beta readiness review of v0.15.0 (2026-11-15). No feature and no CLI change; the scope is bounded to the tasks in this section. The user approved 0.15.1 as a release on 2026-11-15.

### CC-276 · Notion property descriptions fit Notion's 280-character limit · `done` (#386)
**Release:** 0.15.1
**Changelog:** Review's Your call description fits Notion's 280-character limit, and a new space gets the same description as migration 0025.
**Output:** migration 0025 step 1 sets a 270-character Your call description (its 476-character text was refused by Notion when applied to a real space); `notion/prompts/02-databases.md`, `05-ownership.md` and `docs/notion-space-design.md` give a new space that same text instead of the older ones; `tests/notion-property-descriptions.test.ts`.
**Acceptance:**
- [x] Every "Owner: …" description in `notion/prompts/`, `notion/modules/` and `notion/migrations/` is 280 characters or less (test). Evidence: `tests/notion-property-descriptions.test.ts` "every property description the Notion steps set fits Notion's 280-character limit", passing at `v0.15.1` and on CC-367's branch.
- [x] Migration 0025, prompts 02 and 05 and the design doc give Your call the same description, which names Request revision (test). Evidence: the same file's "a new space and migration 0025 give Your call the same description" at `v0.15.1` (0.16.0 removed the Review database, so the test now checks migration 0027's descriptions instead).
- [x] The full suite passes. Evidence: PR #386's CI `test` job passed before its merge, and `npm test` passes on CC-367's branch (output in CC-367's PR).
**Closed by CC-367 (2026-11-20):** merged in #386 (merge `362067b1`), first contained by `v0.15.1`, so it shipped in 0.15.1; its `**Release:**` was 1.0.0, which made `scripts/release.mjs`'s rule 10 count it as later work. Moved here from TASKS.md's 1.0.0 section; the rest of the row is as recorded, its boxes ticked with the evidence above.

### CC-279 · A privacy and data statement, and a security policy · `done` (#389)
**Release:** 0.15.1
**Changelog:** The repository says where your data goes (`PRIVACY.md`) and how to report a security problem (`SECURITY.md`).
**Output:** `PRIVACY.md` at the root: the four services a brain's content reaches (the agent's provider, Notion, Notion AI, GitHub) and when; that the backup is private and checked; that a setup report is a public issue sent only on a yes; that approvals and exclusions bind the `brain` command, the brain folder and linked repositories, not an agent started in an unlinked folder; how captured text with instructions is handled, stated as an instruction and not a guarantee. `SECURITY.md` at the root: private reporting through GitHub's Report a vulnerability, the scope, and that only the latest release is supported. The README links both. Only Markdown changes.
**Acceptance:** `PRIVACY.md` names every service of the review's data-flow table and the limit for unlinked folders; `SECURITY.md` names a private reporting route and no public one; the README links both files; no statement promises more than the product enforces (the reviewer checks each claim against `spec/` or the source); the full suite, the leak check and the PR format check pass. Private vulnerability reporting was turned on in the repository's settings on 2026-11-15.

A patch released before 0.16.0. CC-289 records it; CC-290's contracts come first, then CC-291 to CC-294.

### CC-300 · Task ids planned in an open PR are taken: start --new and pr-format refuse them, next-id prints the free ones · `done` (#400)
**Release:** 0.15.2

**Output:** `scripts/task.mjs start --new` refuses an id that has a row in `build/TASKS.md` on any open task branch, where before it looked only at branch names; `scripts/task.mjs next-id` prints the next free task id, user task id and decision number across `main` and the open task branches; `scripts/pr-format.mjs` fails a PR that adds a task id another open PR uses under another title; AGENTS.md names `next-id`. From PRs #397 and #398, which both planned CC-289 to CC-293 on 2026-11-15 and both passed review.
**Acceptance:** `start --new` refuses an id planned on an open branch and creates no worktree; `next-id` counts `main` and the open branches for all three numbers; `pr-format` rejects a clashing id, accepts a stacked PR that carries the same row, and fails when it can't list the open PRs; the script tests cover each; the full suite and the leak check pass.

## 0.16.0 — One place to decide (decisions 0096, 0098, 0099, 0100, 0101, 0102)

**Recorded by [0096](../decisions/0096-one-place-to-decide.md) (the author's grill of 2026-11-15, Q1 to Q13).** You decide in the Claude Code or Codex session, and Notion is where you read the brain. An attended ingest's yes on the preview takes its items in as `reviewed` and asks, in the same turn, only what removes or replaces something the brain holds (changed values, replaced decisions, merges, answers, unsure flags); additions and links go in directly, cited and listed. Work done without you (a scheduled catch-up, the weekly review, reflection, correction checks, the assistant) only queues questions in `_proposals/`, and each session asks the most consequential, at most five per round. The Review database is removed: a migration applies the decisions already made on its rows, then archives it. The weekly review becomes one Inbox row per week, Kind Weekly. It removes and simplifies, and since [0102](../decisions/0102-one-release-for-0-16-0-and-0-17-0.md) it also holds two features, 0098 §4 to §7 and 0100 (below); 0.15.0 stays the feature cutoff for anything else. Inviting testers (U-28) waits for this release.

**Amended by [0098](../decisions/0098-agree-by-subject-and-check-a-changed-document.md) (the author's grill of 2026-11-16, Q1 to Q9) before it is built.** An attended ingest goes by subject: each part shows a short view of what the brain has on that subject, then what is being added, then its questions, then "accept the rest of this part?". A source with one subject is one part with one yes. CC-307 rewrites CC-295's contract first; CC-296 and CC-298 build that version. The rest of 0098 (a changed document checked as an Inbox row) was 0.17.0's; 0102 moves it here (below).

**Extended by [0099](../decisions/0099-notion-without-decisions-and-questions.md) (the author's grill of 2026-11-16, Q1 to Q9).** The Decisions and Questions databases go too, in the same migration, skills release and template. Decisions and questions stay notes in Git; Notion shows them in the project's Brief ("Open questions and risks") and Decision log, the area's Brief and reports. A question or risk you follow up becomes a task, only when you say "track it" or mark it `Track: Waiting On —`, and Tasks gains a Kind (Task, Question, Risk) with Questions and Risks tabs. The migration archives both databases, and the to-do tidy then offers each open question as a task. Brain Data keeps eleven databases.

**Extended by [0101](../decisions/0101-where-notion-ai-and-the-brain-each-write.md) §4 to §7 (the author's map of 2026-11-16).** Sync no longer applies your edits to brain-owned values in Notion: the next session asks them as one question, because the brain can't tell your edit from an AI's. When the task tracking an open question is Done, or gone, the session asks whether the question is answered. Only the ingest creates a meeting's Tasks: the minutes prompt, Check minutes and Brain Assistant say so. The template and setup say that Brain Assistant and its buttons need Business with Notion credits. The rest of 0101 is 1.0.0's.

**Moved in from 0.17.0 by [0102](../decisions/0102-one-release-for-0-16-0-and-0-17-0.md): [0098](../decisions/0098-agree-by-subject-and-check-a-changed-document.md) §4 to §7 (the author's grill of 2026-11-16).** When you ask in the session for a document to be changed before it goes in (part of it kept, rewritten, corrected), or Keep as document has Only keep filled, the session writes the changed text to a new Inbox row linked to the original. Nothing is taken in yet. You read and edit it in Notion, then say "go" in the session, or set it Ready for the next catch-up. Its items cite the checked text; the original stays in the brain, linked; the row lists what the change dropped or altered. Ready approves the text only: a run without you leaves the row's notes `draft` and its additions and conflicts for your next session (0096 §4). Meetings keep Check minutes and captures keep Tidy capture. It is one of 0.16.0's two features after the 0.15.0 cutoff ([0102](../decisions/0102-one-release-for-0-16-0-and-0-17-0.md)). **One migration and one skills release (CC-331, as 0102's Consequences allow):** CC-311 adds its steps to CC-299's migration, which is unreleased until 0.16.0, and CC-310 cuts the one skills release for CC-298's and its own fork PRs and vendors it, so the template and your brain each run one migration.

**Extended by [0100](../decisions/0100-keep-a-task-as-a-document.md) (the author's grill of 2026-11-16, Q10 to Q15).** Any task can be kept as a document: in the session, or with a **Keep as document** button on Tasks that ticks **Document asked**, which the next catch-up acts on and clears. The task page becomes a source and a whole Document, `reviewed`, linked to its project or area, the question it tracks and the task; then the brain asks what to add from what it holds, in one **From the brain** section at the end, one cited line per item. A run without you keeps the Document and leaves that question for your next session. It needs the Documents module; the flag needs Tasks.

### CC-295 · Contracts for 0096: deciding in the session, no Review database, the weekly review as an Inbox row · `done` (#405)
**Release:** 0.16.0
**Changelog:** development only: the contracts for deciding in the session, the removed Review database and the weekly review's Inbox row.
**Output:** spec only, no code. `spec/capture-and-review.md` §7 is replaced by a short "Deciding in the session" contract: what is asked and what goes in directly (0096 §3); the attended preview's yes and `reviewed` notes (§2); "later"; the session's rounds (at most five, their order, one question per source for a scheduled run's notes and additions, "more" and "enough", §5); how a decision given in chat is recorded in Git with the user's words. §8's unattended rows follow 0096 §4. `spec/cli.md`: which proposal kinds an attended ingest writes and which it applies; `note new` creating `reviewed` notes under the attended yes; the ordered list of waiting proposals (an option of `brain proposal list` or a new command: the contract picks one); `apply` from chat decisions; `sync` without Review rows; §6.1's unattended table. `spec/schema.md`: proposal kinds and states, lifecycle at creation, the Inbox Kind Weekly. `spec/eligibility.md`, `spec/assistant.md` and `spec/weekly-review.md` (the Inbox row, its Done rule, decision lines with no Review link, the Reports offer retired). The transition: what the migration applies from decided rows and in which order; a pending row needs nothing. `docs/notion-space-design.md`: no Review database, Home without its view, the Inbox Kind Weekly, Brain Assistant's skills. Retired text is removed, not kept beside the new; shipped migrations and history keep theirs.
**Acceptance:** every rule of 0096 §2 to §7 is in exactly one contract; no spec file outside history names a Review row, Your call, Grill me or the Submit buttons as current; each removed error code or field is listed with what replaces it, or that nothing does; the transition covers a decided row, a pending row, a row with a requested revision and an older-layout row; the reviewer checks the asked/applied split against 0096 §3 item by item; `npm run test:scripts`, the leak and PR format checks pass.

### CC-296 · The CLI decides in the session: additions and links go in, notes are reviewed on your yes, the waiting questions come in order, no Review rows · `done` (#424)
**Release:** 0.16.0
**Changelog:** You decide what the brain takes in during your Claude Code or Codex session: only changes that replace what the brain holds are asked, and nothing waits for you in Notion.
**Output:** per CC-295's contracts: proposal writing and `apply` on the attended and unattended paths (folding only what goes in with its part's yes; notes become `reviewed` when their part's `review_notes` is approved with that yes, capture-and-review §7.3, so `note new` is unchanged); `proposal list --waiting`; `sync plan --review-transition` and `REVIEW_RETIRED`; `proposal revise` retired; `proposal.schema.json` and its examples where fields change; `sync` no longer renders, publishes or reads Review rows (`src/lib/review-body.ts` and the Review parts of `src/lib/sync.ts` removed, not left unused); the one-time application of decided rows the migration needs; lint and `brain status` lines that named Review; what CC-307's contract asks of the CLI for agreeing by subject (0098 §1 to §3): the optional `subject` on every proposal (schema §11) and its check, one `review_notes` per `source` and `subject` with folding by both, the title with the subject, a deferred proposal keeping `subject`, a held proposal's plain-name subject withheld, group 3 of `--waiting` listing a source's `review_notes` together, and `reject` on an approved or fixed `review_notes` (items turned down, rejected at once; cli §5.6, §5.7, schema §11.3); tests.
**Acceptance:** tests on synthetic brains: an attended ingest with one conflicting fact takes in the other items as `reviewed` and leaves one proposal; an addition and a link go in directly with their passages and are listed; the same input unattended leaves its notes `draft` and queues the conflict, the addition and the link; a mixed waiting list comes in 0096 §5's order, at most five per round; a decision given in chat is applied with its words recorded; `sync` writes no Review row; the transition applies a decided row once and leaves a pending one for the session; a two-subject source decided part by part takes in only the parts said yes to, and a question in one part holds nothing of the other (CC-307's contract); one `approve` with `reject` and `exclude` archives the turned-down note and defers only the later one; no code path reads a Review row except the transition; spec and code agree; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.

### CC-297 · The weekly review is an Inbox row, Kind Weekly (0096 §7) · `done` (#420)
**Release:** 0.16.0
**Changelog:** Each week's review is a row in your Inbox, and you decide its items in your next session.
**Output:** per CC-295's contracts (weekly-review.md, `weekly.schema.json` with `lives: "session"` and its examples): `brain weekly record` and `sync render` publish the week's review as one Inbox row, Kind Weekly, updated in place by a rerun, never taken in as an input; its decision lines point to the session; the row is set to Done when none of its decisions waits; the weekly review's Documents path and the Reports offer removed; tests.
**Acceptance:** tests on synthetic brains: a busy week publishes one Inbox row with Documents and Reports off; a same-week rerun updates that row; no decision line links a Review row; the row turns Done after its last decision; unattended publishing still needs `settings.publish: auto`; a quiet week still writes nothing; spec and code agree; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.

### CC-298 · The skills decide in the session, through the fork (0096 §2 to §5, §7) · `done` (#428)
**Release:** 0.16.0
**Changelog:** Your agent asks what the brain needs from you in short rounds, most important first, and you answer in the chat.
**Output:** a fork PR in ultimate-brain-skills, merged to the fork's `brain/` without a release: CC-310 cuts 0.16.0's one skills release with it and vendors it here (CC-331). This repo's PR records the fork PR and the evidence below. The fork PR changes brain-ingest (by subject, per CC-307: each part's short view of what the brain has, what is added, its questions, then its yes; "later", "enough" and "stop"; nothing written until the parts are answered; one `review_notes` per part with its `subject`, attended or not), brain-catch-up (opens with the waiting questions; step 12 for every brain; one question per source listing its parts with their views, and questions on one subject together in a round, capture-and-review §7.5), brain-apply (chat only), brain-weekly-review (decisions in the session, the Inbox row), brain-reflect and ultimate-assistant (no Review rows), brain-sync and brain-notion-change where they name Review, brain-howto and the Everyday guide. For 0099 (per CC-313): brain-ingest tracks a question or risk as a task on "track it" or the `Track: Waiting On —` token, with Kind Question or Risk, and its to-do tidy offers each open question as a task, one yes at a time; an answer to a tracked question asks to set its task Done; after the 0.16.0 migration the session offers that tidy once (capture-and-review §6.2); brain-connect-notion checks Kind on the default Tasks database, offers it on a mapped one (setup.md §6.2.1) and no longer sets up Knowledge; no skill publishes or reads a Decisions or Questions row; Promote to document is asked in the session. For 0101 (per CC-323): brain-catch-up and "what needs my decision" ask the corrections from Notion as one listed question (accept all, all except …, reject) and the tracked question whose task is Done or gone (answered, drop it, keep it open); brain-sync no longer says it applies your Notion edits, and before its snapshot runs `brain sync tracked` and reads each tracked question's task page by its id (cli §5.14.1); brain-ingest never creates a meeting's Tasks other than its own; brain-setup and brain-howto say that Brain Assistant and its buttons need Business with Notion credits, and brain-setup says to turn off a notetaker's own export of tasks to the brain's Tasks database (0101 §5).
**Acceptance:** the fork PR is merged, and no file in the fork's `brain/` at its merge names a Review row, Your call or Grill me as current (CC-310 checks the vendored snapshot); with the fork PR's skills, one attended catch-up on a synthetic brain with a two-subject meeting shows each part's view and additions, asks the conflict in its part before that part's yes, and leaves nothing in Notion to decide (its summary in the PR, no personal data); no file in the fork's `brain/` at its merge names the Decisions or Questions database as current; on a synthetic brain, "track it" on an open question creates one task with Kind Question and links it to the question, and a meeting line with `Track: Waiting On —` on a risk creates one Waiting On task with Kind Risk; on a synthetic brain, two edits to brain-owned values in a simulated Notion are asked as one question and applied only on the yes, and a tracked question whose task is Done is asked with its three options; the full suite, the leak and PR format checks pass.

### CC-299 · Notion without the Review, Decisions and Questions databases: the migration, Brain Assistant and the Notion design (0096 §6, §7; 0099) · `done` (#431)
**Release:** 0.16.0
**Changelog:** The upgrade applies the decisions you already made on Review rows, then archives the Review database; nothing is deleted.
**Output:** 0.16.0's one Notion migration (the next number; CC-311 adds its steps to it before the release, CC-331) that applies decided rows through CC-296's transition first, then archives the Review database and Home's "What's waiting for you?" view, and adds the Inbox Kind Weekly; `notion/prompts/`, `notion/views-guide.md`, `notion/template-examples.md`, `notion/CHECKLIST.md`; `notion/brain-assistant.md`: Review queue and Grill review retired, Weekly review reads the week's Inbox row and decides nothing; the guides' count of Brain Assistant's skills follows (CC-283 sets its count at its turn); `brain upgrade` names the migration. With the prompts and migration, the Review history block kept in `docs/notion-space-design.md` §3.10 (kept by CC-295 because the Notion prompt tests check the prompts against design §3) is removed. For 0099 (per CC-313), the same migration then runs cli.md §5.14.9's steps: it reads back the Status of every question set in Notion (through CC-314), archives the Decisions and Questions databases, removes their views, relations and toggles (the Knowledge and Archive tabs, the project, area and meeting templates, the Inbox's Decisions column, Promote to document and its checkbox), and adds Kind (Task, Question, Risk) and the Questions and Risks tabs to Tasks, offering Kind on a mapped Tasks database (setup.md §6.2.1), then removes the two keys from `brain.json` and offers the to-do tidy; the Decisions and Questions history blocks kept in `docs/notion-space-design.md` §3 (§3.8, §3.9 and the relation rows on Areas, Projects, Meetings, Inbox and Documents, kept because the Notion prompt tests check the prompts against design §3) go with the prompts, as §3.10's does; `notion/modules/knowledge.md` is retired and `notion/modules/tasks.md` gains Kind; Prep, Write up and Refresh report in `notion/brain-assistant.md` read the project's Brief and Decision log and the Tasks Kind; in `notion/template-examples.md` and `notion/TEMPLATE.md`, the Decisions and Questions Example rows go and the example question becomes a Tasks Example row with Kind Question and Status Waiting On. For 0101 (per CC-323): `notion/minutes-format.md`, Check minutes and Brain Assistant's Instructions say never to create Tasks from a meeting, since the ingest does; the property descriptions of `notion/prompts/05-ownership.md` and the Ownership reminder of `notion/views-guide.md` say an edit to a brain-owned value is asked in your agent session before it reaches the brain; `docs/setup.md` says to turn off a notetaker's own export of tasks to the brain's Tasks database (0101 §5); the template's Start here callout, `notion/TEMPLATE.md`, `notion/brain-assistant.md`'s Setup and `docs/setup.md` say that Brain Assistant and its buttons need Business with Notion credits, with Notion's help page linked and checked when written.
**Acceptance:** this task's steps of the migration run on a fresh duplicate of the 0.15.1 template with synthetic rows: a decided row applied once, a pending row asked in the session afterwards, the database archived, nothing deleted; a question set Answered in Notion is answered in Git before Decisions and Questions are archived, and the Questions and Risks tabs list a tracked synthetic question and risk (recorded in the PR, no personal data); no shipped Notion text outside migrations and history names the Review, Decisions or Questions database as current; no shipped Notion text tells Notion AI to create Tasks from minutes, and the Business-with-credits line is in each of the four places; the full suite, the leak and PR format checks pass.

**Output:** `eval/memory-0.12/score.mjs` (`SEARCHED`: besides "for" and "under", a search verb's "on" or "with" right before an opening quote starts a quoted query, so "searched with care for "…"" still reaches its "for"), `tests/memory-eval-score.test.mjs` and the Forbidden facts paragraph of `eval/memory-0.12/README.md`; nothing else. Found by 0.15.2's two smoke runs: in both trust-34 replies the denial already counted as one ("Nothing I can reach records that …" through CC-223's "nothing" with up to four words, "Nothing the brain returns records that …" likewise); what failed was the narration `searches on "Velmor dispatchers preferred SMS alerts"` (5a5c196) and `I searched both the approved memories and the notes with "…"` (3818866). (The reading put to the user at 5a5c196 named the denial; running the scorer showed the narration, and the findings file says so.) Per decision 0083 §1 item 5, it merges before 0.16.0's smoke run. But …" and a narration followed by the stated preference still fail; at least 5 sentences each way for "on" and "with" (a quoted query that repeats the question is narration; "searched on Harbor: …", "searched with the brain, and …", a quoted query beside a quoted statement, "with … in mind", a ";" before the statement, and a figure the question doesn't hold are statements); the reference run stays 108/108 and `cases.json`'s sha256 is unchanged; both runs rescored offline (copies in /tmp) show Claude Code at 11/12 with only trust-39 failing and Codex unchanged; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, the leak check and the PR format check pass.

## 0.16.1 — First things first, and upgrades with unrelated work (decision 0103)

The author's two fixes from the scope review of 2026-11-17, accepted in [0103](../decisions/0103-first-things-first-and-upgrades-with-unrelated-work.md). The duplicated template makes the manual Brain Assistant setup visible, including its three button bindings and the note to ask Claude Code or Codex to set up and populate the brain. Existing spaces get the same checklist through a migration. An upgrade proceeds around unrelated uncommitted work, preserves file contents and staging state, and reports every actual conflict before writing. No automatic stash or commit of user work, no new knowledge feature, and no change to the 1.0.0 path without Notion AI or the agent rename. The release keeps the normal automated and native smoke gates (0082).

Since [0105](../decisions/0105-domains-on-projects-meetings-and-documents-and-domains-without-type.md) (2026-11-17, an explicit roadmap change): Projects, Meetings and Documents show their Area's Domain through a Notion-only rollup and each Domain page lists them; Domains lose Type. Its Notion steps join CC-337's one migration (0028), its skills changes CC-338's release, and its template steps U-37; U-38 runs the release in the author's brain.

### CC-336 · Upgrade around unrelated uncommitted work and list exact conflicts (0103) · `done` (#444)
**Release:** 0.16.1
**Changelog:** Upgrades leave unrelated drafts and diagrams alone and list the exact conflicting files when they cannot proceed.
**Output:** implement CC-335's upgrade contract in `src/commands/upgrade.ts` and the Git/planning helpers it needs. Determine the full affected paths and dependencies before writes, preserve unrelated file bytes and staged content, commit only upgrade changes, and report every conflict in human, JSON and dry-run output. No automatic stash, broad commit, reset or clean. Correct the README's and `docs/releasing.md`'s lines that say an upgrade refuses any uncommitted change (found by CC-335). Meaningful tests in the upgrade/Git suites use synthetic brains and inspect both working files and the Git index.

The full suite, typecheck, package, leak and PR format checks pass.

### CC-345 · Reports and reflect keep memories out (D1-D3 trust repair) · `done` (#441)
**Release:** 0.16.1
**Changelog:** A report or Brief that rests on a memory, a deleted note or a proposal is now withheld in every brain, a report can no longer link a memory, reflection no longer shows memories, and the brain's AGENTS.md no longer points to the retired Review queue.
**Output:** trust repair under [0077](../decisions/0077-trust-repair-in-0-13-0.md) (eligibility.md §8 already states the rule), found by the LifeOS research review of 2026-11-17 and confirmed on a synthetic brain; the author chose both options on 2026-11-17. **D1:** `withheldView` (`src/lib/suppressions.ts`) skipped the whole derived-content rule when the brain had no suppression record, so a view resting on an out-of-reach, draft or superseded memory, a gone note or a proposal was read, searched and rendered for Notion; the dependency and provenance checks now run in every brain (only the `stale` case needs a record), and a view never rests on a memory: it has no caller to judge reach by and memories never reach Notion (0008). `brain report new` refuses a link to a memory (`INPUT_INVALID`). **D2:** `brain reflect --candidates` (and the `near-duplicate` lint and the weekly review, which share it) leave memories out entirely; duplicate memories stay with the closing review's `supersede` (memory.md). **D3:** the brain template's rules block names the session decision (0096) instead of the retired Notion Review queue, and the retired-database text check covers `templates/`. Specs first: cli.md §5.13.1 (no suppression-record condition; memory dependency), §5.18 (memory links refused), §5.32 (no memories), eligibility.md §2 and §8 rule 1. No migration and no skills-fork change.
**Acceptance:** on a synthetic brain **with no suppression record**: a report linking a reviewed memory (written directly, as an older report would be) is `withheld` by `query --read` with `cause: dependency`, absent from `query --candidates --types view`, withheld from `sync render`/the plan and titled `null` in the JSON index; the same for a report linking a deleted note and one listing a proposal; a report resting only on eligible notes is unchanged; `report new` and a refresh refuse a body that links a memory; `reflect --candidates` returns no memory as member, name or reference, a draft memory and another project's memory included, and lint gives no `near-duplicate` for two memories; the template has no "Review queue" line and the retired-database check reads `templates/`. The existing derived-content, report-excluded and restricted-lineage tests pass unchanged. The full suite, typecheck, package, leak and PR format checks pass.

## 0.16.2 — Beta fixes (decision 0106)

The first patch of the beta, from the author's 1.0.0 opening scope review of 2026-11-18, accepted in [0106](../decisions/0106-beta-on-0-16-x-patches-0-17-0-and-0-18-0-before-1-0-and-the-launch-bar.md). The beta starts on 0.16.2 ([0108](../decisions/0108-beta-invitations-wait-for-0-16-2.md), amending 0106 §2; 0.16.1 before it: migration 0029 changes what a tester meets first): U-31 after U-41, then U-32 from `v0.16.2`, then U-28 (five English-speaking testers with Notion Business and credits, reached directly), and the beta runs on 0.16.x patches: beta fixes and launch-readiness polish, no new feature (§1). A patch is cut when at least one fix a tester can see is merged, at most about once a week, each with the native smoke run and `results.md` read before `scripts/release.mjs` (docs/releasing.md); a trust or data-loss fix ships at once. Later 0.16.x patches get their rows from triaged `beta-setup` and `dogfood` issues. The author added two items the same day, both polish under §1: a New task button on Home (§12, CC-355) and the minutes layout (§13, CC-356, amending 0079's layout only). 0.16.2 has one Notion migration, 0029, for CC-350's, CC-355's and CC-356's steps; U-41 applies it to the published template and U-42 to the author's brain, as U-37 and U-38 did. The fork changes of CC-267, CC-269, CC-283, CC-350 and CC-356 may share one skills release. The same day the author renamed Meetings' Brain Status to the Inbox's names, New, Ready and Done, with Skipped kept on Meetings only ([0109](../decisions/0109-meetings-use-the-inbox-s-status-names.md), a named exception to 0106 §1): CC-359 records it, CC-360 builds the contract and the CLI, CC-361, stacked on it, the Notion and fork side; CC-361's step joins migration 0029 and its fork changes the same skills release.

### CC-267 · The catch-up's publish runs in a sub-agent with a fresh context (gate problem 9) · `done` (#458)
**Release:** 0.16.2
**Changelog:** a catch-up writes its Notion pages and Tasks in a fresh context, so a long run no longer runs out of context while it publishes.
**Output:** from CC-264's breakdown: the gate's first-meeting publish made 34 Notion calls with 250k to 350k tokens of context, about 70% of the second run's cost, and the session compacted itself mid-publish, against decision 0043's bar that a catch-up never runs out of context. Contract first (brain-catch-up step 4.1, with the rule that a sub-agent never writes amended for this one): the Pages and Tasks writes (4.1.1, 4.1.2) run in one sub-agent given the brain path, `plan.json`, the render and the run's temp folder; it reads brain-ingest references/notion.md "Publish" and brain-sync's Push itself, writes, records with `sync record`, and returns the counts and failures; the main run keeps the other publish steps. A fork PR.
**Acceptance:** a synthetic catch-up with simulated Notion (eval/unattended-0.7.0's pattern) over one meeting, setup's leftover rows and a report, before and after: the same pages and Tasks written and recorded, the same report, the cost recorded against the before run (about +7 to 10% in the two pairs is accepted by the author, 0043's no-compaction bar being the reason) and a peak context under 200k tokens with no compaction; the fork PR's checks pass; the leak check passes.
**Acceptance amended (2026-11-18):** the author accepted the before/after's higher cost: "a lower cost" became "the cost recorded against the before run", and "the same pages" became "the same pages and Tasks" (the after run creates the before run's Tasks, the owner's only).
**Moved by [0106](../decisions/0106-beta-on-0-16-x-patches-0-17-0-and-0-18-0-before-1-0-and-the-launch-bar.md) (2026-11-18):** from 1.0.0 to 0.16.2 (its `**Release:**` was 1.0.0; the rest of the row is as recorded). A beta fix for long catch-ups (0106 §3); its fork change may share 0.16.2's one skills release.

### CC-269 · The CLI turns Notion's query replies into the snapshot · `done` (#455)
**Release:** 0.16.2
**Changelog:** a sync no longer copies every Notion row by hand into its snapshot: the CLI converts the rows itself.
**Output:** from CC-264's breakdown: in the gate the agent wrote the snapshot of every row as a script by hand (about 8.8k output tokens, one call of 13% of the first run's cost, over a minute). Contract first (spec/cli.md §5.14, brain-sync references/formats.md): `brain sync snapshot --input <files>` takes the Notion query replies as saved files and writes `snapshot.json` with formats.md's normalized values, leaving out the `Example · ` rows; the skill saves each reply to the run's temp folder instead of retyping it. A fork PR for brain-sync. *Scope clarified with the coordinator (2026-11-18):* the rows that are no query reply (the tracked tasks read by page id, the `wording` objects) are passed to the same command as a rows file in today's row format, with no new field, told apart from a reply by its shape and joined by page only for a tracked task or a wording object; a property with two different values is `INPUT_INVALID`, so the CLI is the only writer of `snapshot.json`.
**Acceptance:** tests on synthetic query replies of each database (every property type of formats.md's table, a relation, an empty value, an example row, a paged reply) give the snapshot the skill writes today; `sync plan` takes it unchanged; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.
**Moved by [0106](../decisions/0106-beta-on-0-16-x-patches-0-17-0-and-0-18-0-before-1-0-and-the-launch-bar.md) (2026-11-18):** from 1.0.0 to 0.16.2 (its `**Release:**` was 1.0.0; the rest of the row is as recorded). A beta fix for the cost of a sync (0106 §3); its fork change may share 0.16.2's one skills release.
