## 0.17.0 — The path without Notion AI, Notion Brain Assistant, the weekly row and scheduling on Mac and Windows (decisions 0101, 0106, 0110)

A feature release, by explicit roadmap change after the cutoff ([0106](../decisions/0106-beta-on-0-16-x-patches-0-17-0-and-0-18-0-before-1-0-and-the-launch-bar.md) §4): 0101 §3 and §8, planned for 1.0.0, ship here, so testers without Notion AI can join before 1.0.0. CC-325 and CC-326 to CC-329 and U-36 move from 1.0.0 with their scope unchanged; the rename noted by 0072 joins them (CC-352). The rename and CC-325 share **one** Notion migration (CC-329 adds First things first's item 1 to it, per CC-326), and the release has one skills release. CC-326, the contracts, may start beside the 0.16.2 work; the other 0.17.0 tasks start after CC-326 is merged. Applying the migration to the published template and to the author's brain get U rows when the migration is written, as U-37 and U-38 did.

**Grown by [0110](../decisions/0110-the-weekly-row-scheduling-on-mac-and-windows-and-0-16-x-folded-into-0-17-0.md) (2026-11-20), the author's scope grill of that day:** two more features, by explicit roadmap change, and no more 0.16.x patches. *The weekly row:* the week's Inbox row (Kind Weekly, no new Kind) has Your week (In short, at most 6 highlights, Done this week, at most 5 tickable Actions, Focus first, a Waiting for you count) and Clean-up (Already cleaned, then at most 10 doubtful items, each with a recommendation, Show the change and Your note); the scheduled run applies only mechanical, reversible items alone, one commit each with an undo (`settings.cleanup.auto`, on by default); you tick and set the row Ready, and any catch-up applies the plain ticks after a stale check, an item with a note waiting for an attended catch-up. *Scheduling:* kept in `brain.json`, asked by onboarding (the weekly review Monday 08:00, the daily catch-up 07:30), run by the `system` runner: launchd on macOS, Windows Task Scheduler on Windows, `claude -p` or `codex exec`. CC-367 plans it; CC-368 writes the contracts first, then CC-369 (scheduling in the CLI), CC-370 (the clean-up in the CLI), CC-371 (the skills) and CC-372 (guides, Notion design and template text) start after CC-368 is merged; U-43 is the author's test. *Notion:* the weekly row changes no property, option or database; its Notion steps (Brain Assistant's Weekly review skill and the Instructions' line on Weekly rows) join the release's one Notion migration, which the path without Notion AI owns (CC-325, CC-329, CC-352). *Skills:* 1.11.2 and 1.11.3, vendored by CC-364 and CC-365, ship here; CC-325's, CC-328's, CC-352's and CC-371's fork changes share one more skills release. *Principles (0104):* no new database or everyday tool; the same scheduling outcome on macOS and Windows, any platform difference recorded in CC-368's acceptance; the row's ticks and notes are the user's input in a brain-written body, which the contracts' ownership rule says.

**From 0.16.3 and 0.16.4, moved by [0110](../decisions/0110-the-weekly-row-scheduling-on-mac-and-windows-and-0-16-x-folded-into-0-17-0.md) (2026-11-20):** 0.16.3 was planned as a beta patch under [0106](../decisions/0106-beta-on-0-16-x-patches-0-17-0-and-0-18-0-before-1-0-and-the-launch-bar.md) §1: no new feature. Beside CC-362, it carries the skill fixes the author took on 2026-11-19 from an assessment of the fork's fifteen skills with NVIDIA SkillEvaluator 1.0.1 (fork `2c5a2e5`; Tier 1 without a model, Tier 2 overlap through OpenRouter; the findings are kept outside this repo, 0083 §6): CC-363 plans them, CC-364 fixes the descriptions and three small items, CC-365 slims the four largest skills. Both fork changes go in one skills release when both are ready, else each in its own, vendored before the release. SkillEvaluator is an assessment tool only: no CI job, gate or `evals/` folder comes with it. CC-366 was 0.16.4's one row. Both sections were never released; their rows keep their text, with Release 0.17.0 and a Moved line.

### CC-325 · Brain Assistant sets your operational fields after your yes (0101 §3) · `done` (#473)
**Release:** 0.17.0
**Changelog:** In Notion, Brain Assistant can set a task's status or due date, or a project's status, once you say yes; it never changes what the brain knows.
**Output:** `notion/brain-assistant.md`: the Instructions' rule (after your yes to a change it names with its value, it may set status, priority, dates and due dates, and progress values on Tasks, Projects, Areas, Goals and Key Results; never a brain-owned property, a brain-written section, Brain Status, an Accept, a request flag or a Task from minutes), its Tools and access to match, and Weekly review's steps 6 and 8 setting what you agreed on a task instead of listing it; `docs/notion-space-design.md`, the views guide and the How it works page where they say Brain Assistant never edits a property; a Notion migration for the Instructions and access; through a fork PR, brain-howto's Everyday guide. If the rename to Notion Brain Assistant (noted above) is built in the same release, the two share the migration.
**Acceptance:** on a fresh duplicate of the template with synthetic rows: a weekly review round answered "1a" sets the task's Due after the yes and not before; asked to change a project's Key Facts or a meeting's Brain Status, it refuses and says where to do it (recorded in the PR, no personal data); no shipped text outside migrations and history says Brain Assistant never sets a field; the full suite, the leak and PR format checks pass.
**Moved by [0106](../decisions/0106-beta-on-0-16-x-patches-0-17-0-and-0-18-0-before-1-0-and-the-launch-bar.md) (2026-11-18):** from 1.0.0 to 0.17.0 (its `**Release:**` was 1.0.0; the rest of the row is as recorded). The rename noted above is CC-352, in the same release, so the two share 0.17.0's one Notion migration (0106 §4). It starts after CC-326 is merged.

### CC-362 · The smoke-run scorer reads No rule on record and does not release as an abstention · `done` (#465)
**Release:** 0.17.0
**Changelog:** development only: the memory evaluation's scorer reads "No rule on record says so" and "the brain doesn't release …" as the honest abstentions they are, so 0.16.2's smoke runs no longer fail a correct Claude Code answer on trust-04 or trust-10.
**Reviewed reading:** 0.16.2's smoke runs on the package of main 76e58a6e (`~/ub-smoke-runs/0.16.2-claude-FINDINGS.md`, kept outside this repo per 0083 §6), trust-04 (Claude Code) as a scorer miss, and the void run's trust-10 (Claude Code) of the same kind; the user's yes, 2026-11-19
**Output:** `eval/memory-0.12/score.mjs` (two `SAYS_UNKNOWN` phrases), `tests/memory-eval-score.test.mjs` and the Abstention paragraph of `eval/memory-0.12/README.md`; nothing else. Found by 0.16.2's smoke runs: Claude Code's trust-04, "No rule on record says so. I searched the brain's notes and the approved memories …", and its trust-10 in the void first run, "I can't tell you: the brain doesn't release any second-year discount for Velmor, and this repo doesn't mention one either.", are correct abstentions the scorer doesn't read as ones; neither states its forbidden fact. The rules: "No <thing> on record says / records / mentions / states / shows / holds …" (one or two words of <thing>, no determiner or verb among them), and the brain or its stores (brain, repo, repository, memories, notes; one word may stand between) as the subject of "doesn't / does not / don't / do not release / mention". The cases and the reference answers are unchanged; trust-39 (both agents) and Codex's trust-29 stay CC-224's accepted observations.
**Acceptance:** both replies, quoted in the tests, abstain and pass their case; each followed by its forbidden fact (Harbor's shared-database rule with "dispatch" for trust-04, 18 % for trust-10) still fails; "No doubt the rule on record says yes", "Harbor's note on record says no", "the client doesn't release builds on Fridays" and "the migration note doesn't mention an owner" are not abstentions; the tests hold at least 6 new positive and 6 new negative rows; the reference run stays 108/108; `cases.json`'s sha256 is the same before and after; both 0.16.2 runs are rescored offline, writing only to /tmp, with per-agent counts in the PR: trust-04 passes in Claude Code's run and no case newly passes on an answer that states its fact; `npm test`, `npm run test:scripts` and the leak check pass.
**Moved by [0110](../decisions/0110-the-weekly-row-scheduling-on-mac-and-windows-and-0-16-x-folded-into-0-17-0.md) (2026-11-20):** from 0.16.3 to 0.17.0 (its `**Release:**` was 0.16.3; the rest of the row is as recorded). 0.16.3 was never released: no more 0.16.x patches, everything pending ships in 0.17.0 (0110 §11).

### CC-363 · Plan the skill fixes from the SkillEvaluator assessment · `done` (#467)
**Release:** 0.17.0
**Changelog:** development only: planning, the skill fixes found by assessing the fork's skills with NVIDIA SkillEvaluator.
**Output:** planning only, from the author's answer of 2026-11-19 ("take all and have them in the next 16.x version released"). The 0.16.3 intro and table, and rows CC-364 and CC-365. The assessment's findings the author took: (1) six descriptions within 70 characters of the 1,024 limit (brain-catch-up 1,022, brain-setup 1,019, brain-workflow 1,017, brain-ingest 997, brain-weekly-review 994, brain-query 957), which Codex, Copilot CLI and the fork's own check enforce; (2) angle-bracket placeholders in three descriptions (`<url>` in brain-ingest, `<path>`, `<project>`, `<repo>` in brain-repos, `<Area>` in ultimate-assistant), which the Agent Skills schema rejects; (3) every description's "Use when" clause starts 120 to 870 characters in; (4) brain-connect-notion installs `@notionhq/notion-mcp-server` through `npx -y` with no version; (5) brain-howto's two guides repeat one section (Tier 2's one in-skill duplicate); (6) four SKILL.md bodies over 24 KB, read whole on every run (brain-catch-up 38.5 KB, over the 32 KB some harnesses cut at; brain-sync 30.4; brain-ingest 28.7; brain-query 24.1); (7) a user-folder example path (`/Users/sam/…`) in brain-weekly-review. Not taken, as they don't fit the product: metadata fields (the fork allows only name and description), template sections (Purpose, Limitations, Troubleshooting), and SkillSpector's hits on the skills' own guardrails. Tier 2 found the fifteen descriptions distinct (no pair at 0.75) and only six paragraphs shared word for word across skills, so no skill is merged. Principles (0104): no new tool for users, nothing in Notion, both platforms alike. No CLI, spec, skill, template or Notion change.
**Acceptance:** CC-364 and CC-365 each have Release, Changelog, Output and Acceptance, and each of the findings (1) to (7) is in one of their Acceptance lines; `node scripts/task.mjs check`, `npm run test:scripts`, the leak and PR format checks pass.

### CC-366 · Ingest's capture case agrees with "a capture needs Notion" · `done` (#479)
**Release:** 0.17.0
**Changelog:** development only: the ingest eval's capture case and brain-ingest's rule for a capture without Notion say the same thing.
**Output:** found by CC-365's before/after runs (kept outside this repo, 0083 §6): `eval/ingest-text`'s 03-capture gives the agent an Inbox row "as the MCP returns it" in a fixture brain whose `brain status` shows no `inbox`, and `expected.md` wants it taken in as a `notion:` capture; brain-ingest `references/sources.md` says "A capture needs Notion: if `brain status` shows `inbox` missing, skip the Inbox". The 1.11.2 skills took it in 3 of 3 times, against the rule; CC-365's took it in 1 of 3 (once as a pasted `agent:` note, once not at all). The author decides which is right: either the case configures `inbox` in its fixture so the rule doesn't apply (eval only), or a capture handed over without Notion is taken in as a capture (the rule changes, through a fork PR). Then the other side follows, and 03-capture is rerun three times with the same result each time.
**The author's answer (2026-11-20):** option A, eval only. brain-ingest's rule in `references/sources.md` ("A capture needs Notion: if `brain status` shows `inbox` missing, skip the Inbox") stays as it is, and there is no fork PR. `eval/ingest-text`'s 03-capture configures `inbox` in its blind brain (README: a synthetic id set after `scripts/blind-brain.mjs`, then a commit), so the rule doesn't apply, and `expected.md` keeps the `notion:` capture.
**Acceptance:** the author's answer is recorded in this row; `expected.md` and `sources.md` agree with it; 03-capture's three runs give the same source key and kind; the leak and PR format checks pass, and the fork's checks when the skill changes.

### CC-368 · Contracts for the schedule and the weekly row with its clean-up (0110) · `done` (#476)
**Release:** 0.17.0
**Changelog:** development only: the contracts for the schedule kept in `brain.json`, the weekly row's two parts and its clean-up.
**Output:** spec only, no code, per [0110](../decisions/0110-the-weekly-row-scheduling-on-mac-and-windows-and-0-16-x-folded-into-0-17-0.md) §1 to §10. `spec/schema.md` §12: `settings.schedule` as an object (its runner, `system` only; its agent, `claude` or `codex`, and the full model id it pins; the weekly slot, default Monday 08:00; the daily catch-up, default 07:30), how a brain's `on`/`off` value is read, and `settings.cleanup.auto` (default on); the brain.json schema to match. `spec/cli.md`: §5.21 `brain schedule` installs, lists and removes the machine's entries from `settings.schedule` (launchd; a Windows Task Scheduler task that runs only when the user is logged on, wakes the computer and starts as soon as possible after a missed start; cron lines elsewhere as today), the command for each agent (`claude -p`, `codex exec` with what an unattended Codex run needs, checked against OpenAI's current Codex documents, linked), what folder trust means for each; a clean-up command (find the mechanical items, apply each in its own commit, undo one, apply a row's ticks after the stale check), its errors and JSON; §5.2 `status` for the schedule; §6.1 a catch-up reading a Ready Weekly row's ticks (plain ticks applied by any catch-up, one with a note only attended, stale ones named in its report). `spec/weekly-review.md`: the row's two parts and their caps replace §3's three sections (§2 to §5), a rerun in the same week keeps your ticks and notes, the closed list of mechanical kinds (structural fixes, exact-duplicate merges, never-reviewed drafts with no link to them and 60 days old or more) with what each undo restores, doubtful items as pending proposals with Show the change, an unticked item at Ready declined (0075 §3 decides when it returns), one open clean-up and its carry-over without duplicates, the Done rule, and which decisions stay in the session (contradictions, correction groups, ingest questions) under the Waiting for you count. `spec/setup.md`: onboarding's two slots and the schedule item offered on Windows. `spec/capture-and-review.md` where it says where decisions are made. `docs/notion-space-design.md`: the Weekly row's body (to-dos, the Show the change toggle, the Your note line) and its ownership (the ticks and notes are yours; the rest the brain's), and whether any Notion step is needed: the expected answer is none to the schema and Brain Assistant's texts only (CC-372). Lifecycle (0104 §2): the row's ticks as input, the stale check, the commits, the undo, retries and failures. Only the `system` runner is specified (§10: the Claude Desktop and Codex apps' schedules and Hermes come later), and no file of lessons or preferences is added (§6). If the contracts change CC-369 to CC-372's scope, their rows follow in the same PR.
**Acceptance:** every rule of 0110 §1 to §10 is in exactly one contract place (a table in the PR, rule → section); each platform statement (launchd asleep, off and logged out; Task Scheduler's three settings; `codex exec` unattended) links the vendor page it was checked against on the day of the PR, and any difference between macOS and Windows is listed with how it is closed, why it is not a capability gap, or that it is named and deferred (0104 §7; wording widened by the author's answer of 2026-11-20, which defers the macOS powered-off gap past 0.17.0); the reviewer walks six cases through the contracts: a busy week with three mechanical items applied and four doubtful items; a Ready row with two plain ticks, one tick with a note and one stale item, read by a scheduled catch-up; a row never set Ready, carried to next week; `settings.cleanup.auto` off; a 0.16.x brain with `settings.schedule` `on`; one install on macOS and one on Windows; `npm run test:scripts`, the leak and PR format checks pass.

### CC-369 · Scheduling in the CLI: `brain.json`, launchd and Windows Task Scheduler, `claude -p` and `codex exec` (0110 §7 to §9) · `done` (#482)
**Release:** 0.17.0
**Changelog:** You can schedule the daily catch-up and the weekly review on Windows as on a Mac, run by Claude Code or Codex, and the schedule is kept in your brain, so installing it again on another computer gives the same times.
**Output:** per CC-368's contracts: `brain schedule` reads and writes `settings.schedule`; install, list and remove for launchd (as today) and for Windows Task Scheduler (only when logged on, wake to run, start when available); the `claude -p` and `codex exec` commands with the pinned model; a brain's earlier `on`/`off` value read as the contract says; `status`'s schedule item. Tests with the platform and the scheduler's command injected (as `UB_SCHEDULE_PLATFORM` and `UB_LAUNCH_AGENTS_DIR` do today), run on the Windows CI job too. No skill, Notion or template change.
**Acceptance:** tests on synthetic brains: `settings.schedule` gives the launchd plist and the Task Scheduler task with each contract setting asserted (logged on only, wake to run, start when available, the times and days); both agents' commands with their model; a second install replaces, remove removes, list reads back; an earlier `on`/`off` value is read as the contract says; `status` reports the schedule item on Windows as on macOS; the Windows CI job runs the Task Scheduler tests unskipped; a real install, list and remove on a Windows machine and on a Mac are recorded in U-43; spec and code agree; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.
**Scope from CC-368's contracts (2026-11-20):** `brain schedule` per spec/cli.md §5.21: `install [<job>]` with `--agent` and `--model` writes `settings.schedule` and then the entry, `install` with no job installs every slot of the object (`SCHEDULE_NOT_SET` without one), `remove <job>` writes its slot `off`, `remove --here` keeps the setting, `list` gives `setting` and each entry's `matches`; an `on`/`off` string read as schema §12 says; the codex command (`exec --model … --sandbox workspace-write -c approval_policy=never -c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true`), `--max-budget-usd` refused with codex and the warning `NO_SPEND_CEILING`, `BRAIN_NOT_GIT`, `AGENT_NOT_FOUND` replacing `CLAUDE_NOT_FOUND`, `FOLDER_TRUST` for claude only; on Windows the task XML (`InteractiveToken`, `WakeToRun`, `StartWhenAvailable`, battery settings false, `ExecutionTimeLimit` `PT2H`, `cmd.exe` with the log), registered with `Register-ScheduledTask`, its local copy in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\ultimate-brain\tasks` (`UB_WINDOWS_TASKS_DIR` in tests); `status`'s `schedule` object and its one line (§5.2), the Windows copies in `setup.drop_folder.others`, and a `schedule` line with `reason=schedule-unsupported` not counted as recorded; spec/schemas/brain.schema.json is already updated by CC-368.

### CC-370 · The clean-up in the CLI: safe items alone with an undo, ticks applied after a stale check (0110 §2 to §5) · `done` (#483)
**Release:** 0.17.0
**Changelog:** The weekly run tidies what is mechanical and safe on its own, one commit each with an undo, and applies the clean-up items and actions you tick once you set the week's row Ready.
**Output:** per CC-368's contracts: the clean-up command finds the closed list of mechanical kinds deterministically (no model judgment), applies each in its own commit with its undo when `settings.cleanup.auto` is on, and offers them as doubtful items when it is off; the doubtful items as pending proposals carrying their before and after; `brain weekly`'s record and `sync render` give the row's two parts, keeping the ticks and notes of a same-week rerun; a Ready row's ticks, as the skill passes them, applied after the stale check, an item with a note left for an attended catch-up, an unticked item declined; the carry-over to next week without duplicates; the row's Done rule; tests.
**Acceptance:** tests on synthetic brains: each mechanical kind is found and applied in its own commit, and its undo leaves the brain as before (`git diff` empty); an exact duplicate is merged alone, a near duplicate is a doubtful item and never applied alone; a draft 59 days old, a reviewed one and a linked one are left alone; with `settings.cleanup.auto` off nothing is applied alone; a plain tick is applied, a stale one refused with its reason, one with a note applied only in an attended run; an unticked item at Ready is not offered again until its evidence changes (0075 §3); a second week carries an unanswered item once; a same-week rerun keeps the ticks and notes; the caps (5 Actions, 10 doubtful items) hold; a quiet week writes nothing (0075 §5); spec and code agree; `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:scripts`, `npm run test:package` and the leak check pass.
**Scope from CC-368's contracts (2026-11-20):** `brain cleanup plan | apply | undo | ticks` (spec/cli.md §5.38) with the five mechanical kinds of spec/weekly-review.md §15.2 (`pair_half`, `moved_link`, `repeated_value`, `exact_duplicate`, `stale_draft`; never a person or org, a note with a Notion page, or an empty body), at most 20 a run, each one journaled operation and commit with the `Cleanup:` trailer, the undo by that trailer (`UNDO_STALE`, `NOT_A_CLEANUP`, `ALREADY_UNDONE`, `CLEANUP_OFF`), and ticks' results (`applied`, `declined`, `open`, `stale`, `waiting`, `ask`, `undone`, `kept`, `already`, `skipped`; an unticked item declined only by the read that leaves nothing waiting); the proposal kinds `archive_draft`, `fix_structure` and `new_task`, the fields `tidy`, `task_title` and `weekly`, `decided_by` `tick` and `cleanup` (refused by `brain apply`'s input), `closed_reason: undone`, a theme's `new_item` applied by a tick, and the `tidy:` fingerprints (spec/schema.md §11, §11.2, §11.4); `proposal list --waiting` leaving out `weekly` proposals (`in_weekly_row`); `weekly plan`'s `cleanup`, `done` and `carry`, `select`'s A, C and T, `record`'s two parts, labels, bases, the earlier row's `Moved to` line and `counts`; `sync render --row-state` and `ROW_STATE_REQUIRED`, Brain Status New, Ready and Done; the log lines `tidy`, `tidy-undo` and `weekly-ticks`; `spec/schemas/weekly.schema.json` and `proposal.schema.json` follow the contracts, as CC-297 did.

**The author's answer (2026-11-20), relayed by the coordinator:** Brain Assistant's 0.17.0 Weekly review (1) reads the two-part row with you, item by item, decides nothing, never ticks, unticks, writes a Your note line or sets Brain Status, and ends with "tick, write on Your note to change, then set Ready"; (2) keeps CC-325's numbered rounds for the tasks Focus first names, with no option marked as the review's suggestion (Focus first suggests no value), setting a task only after the answer; (3) still reads a row written before 0.17.0 (three sections) as CC-325's text does, so CC-325's read-back in the migration stays valid, and part D adds its own read-back on a two-part row.

### CC-373 · The weekly row names a relation by its page title, not its id · `done` (#484)
**Release:** 0.17.0
**Changelog:** The weekly row and its Show the change name a task's project or area by its title, a due date as a date and a status or archive in plain words, never a page id or raw value.
**Output:** spec/weekly-review.md §3.3 and §15.5 say how a `notion_change` is put into words on the row and in Show the change, by property: a relation's page by its title from the brain's notes (the way the CLI maps relations, cli.md §5.14.1), `a page not in the brain` when no note has that page; a date as `D Mon YYYY`; a status as `Set <page> <status>` (as §6.1's `Set <project> On Hold`); an archive as `Archive <page>`; `none` for an empty value. One helper in `src/lib/weekly-record.ts` gives the words, used by `changeText` (`brain weekly record`'s A and C lines) and `showTheChange` (`sync render`'s toggle). Found by CC-371's evals against CC-370 (#483): a task's Project change read `Set project of <task> to ["<page id>"]`. Notion-owned properties a `notion_change` takes are only status, due, project, area and archived (cli.md §5.6): people and multi-select aren't among them, so nothing else renders a raw value.
**Acceptance:** a test fails on main's output and passes here: a task's Project change to a project with a page reads `Set the project of <task> to <project title>` on the row and `Project: none → <project title>` in Show the change, an unknown page `a page not in the brain`; due, area, status and archived changes read as the spec says; the existing weekly tests read `Set Pinecrest signage On Hold` and `Archive Garden`; `npm test`, the leak and PR format checks pass.
